  "short_description": {
    "id": "Púca adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Kelt, Inggris, dan Kepulauan Channel yang dapat membawa untung atau sial dan berubah wujud menjadi kuda, kambing, kucing, anjing, atau kelinci.",
    "en": "The púca is a creature of Celtic, English and Channel Islands folklore that can bring good or bad luck and shifts into a horse, goat, cat, dog or hare.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "puca-c01",
      "puca-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Púca (Irlandia/Irish: roh atau hantu; jamak púcaí), juga puca, pwca, pooka, phouka, dan puck, adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Kelt (Celtic), Inggris (English), dan Kepulauan Channel (Channel Islands). Ia dianggap membawa nasib baik maupun buruk dan dapat menolong atau menyusahkan komunitas desa dan pesisir. Púca dapat berbulu gelap atau putih, dan dikatakan berubah wujud menjadi kuda, kambing, kucing, anjing, dan kelinci, atau menyamar sebagai manusia dengan ciri hewan seperti telinga atau ekor. Kata púca dalam bahasa Irlandia berasal dari bahasa Inggris Pertengahan (Middle English) puck; dalam mitologi Wales (Welsh) ia disebut pwca dan dalam bahasa Cornwall (Cornish) bucca.",
      "en": "The púca (Irish for spirit or ghost; plural púcaí), also puca, pwca, pooka, phouka and puck, belongs to Celtic, English and Channel Islands folklore. Bringing either good or bad fortune, it might aid or trouble farming and seafaring communities alike. Púcaí may have dark or white coats, and were said to change into horses, goats, cats, dogs and hares, or to take a human guise with animal traits such as ears or a tail. The Irish word comes from Middle English puck; in Welsh myth the creature is the pwca and in Cornish the bucca.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "puca-c01",
        "puca-c02",
        "puca-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Thomas Keightley, seorang anak dekat Killarney menceritakan bahwa púca dahulu banyak, berwatak jahat, berwarna hitam, dan muncul sebagai anak kuda liar dengan rantai tergantung, mencelakai pelancong yang lengah. Púca biasa membujuk manusia menunggangi punggungnya, memberi perjalanan liar yang menakutkan, lalu menjatuhkan orang itu kembali di tempat semula. Menurut artikel de, wujud kesukaannya adalah kuda poni hitam; ia sering menjatuhkan \"tamunya\" ke jurang atau rawa lalu menghilang sambil tertawa, dan biji-bijian yang tertinggal di ladang setelah Samhain menjadi milik púca, sehingga Halloween juga disebut \"Púca night\".",
      "en": "Thomas Keightley records a boy near Killarney saying the pookas were once plentiful, wicked, black-looking things that came as wild colts hung with chains and harmed careless travellers. The púca likes to coax people onto its back for a wild, frightening ride before dropping them back where it found them. According to the de article its favourite form is a black pony; it often throws its 'guest' off a cliff or into a bog and vanishes laughing, and any grain left in the fields after Samhain belongs to the púca, so Halloween is also called 'Púca night'.",

      "quote": "(Irish for spirit/ghost; plural púcaí), puca (Old English for goblin), also pwca, pooka, pookah, phouka, and puck, is a creature of Celtic, English, and Channel Islands folklore.       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Púca (Irlandia, Irish; púcaí), juga pwca, pooka, phouka, puck, makhluk cerita rakyat Kelt (Celtic), Inggris (English), Kepulauan Channel (Channel Islands); pembawa untung dan sial; di Irlandia dan Britania.",
        "en": "The púca (Irish; púcaí), also pwca, pooka, phouka, puck, is a creature of Celtic, English and Channel Islands folklore, bringing good and bad fortune."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c02",
      "source_id": "puca-s1",
      "quote": "Púcaí can have dark or white fur or hair. The creatures were said to be shape-changers that could take the appearance of horses, goats, cats, dogs, and hares. They may also take a human disguise, which includes various animal features, such as animal ears or a tail.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Púcaí berbulu gelap atau putih, berubah menjadi kuda, kambing, kucing, anjing, kelinci, atau menyamar manusia bertelinga atau berekor hewan.",
        "en": "Púcaí have dark or white fur and shift into horses, goats, cats, dogs, hares, or humans with animal ears or tails."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c03",
      "source_id": "puca-s1",
      "quote": "The Irish word púca is of English origin, the etymon being Middle English puck. The púca has counterparts throughout the Celtic and Germanic cultures of Northwest Europe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Irlandia púca dari Inggris Pertengahan (Middle English) puck; dalam mitologi Wales (Welsh) pwca, Cornwall (Cornish) bucca.",
        "en": "The Irish word púca comes from Middle English puck; Welsh pwca, Cornish bucca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c04",
      "source_id": "puca-s1",
      "quote": "were wicked-minded, black-looking, bad things ... that would come in the form of wild colts, with chains hanging about them\", and that did much to harm unwary travellers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Thomas Keightley, anak dekat Killarney bercerita púca jahat dan hitam, muncul sebagai anak kuda liar berantai, mencelakai pelancong.",
        "en": "Per Thomas Keightley, a boy near Killarney said pookas were wicked black things coming as wild colts with chains, harming travellers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c05",
      "source_id": "puca-s1",
      "quote": "They are commonly said to entice humans to take a ride on their back, giving the rider a wild and terrifying journey before dropping the unlucky person back at the place they were taken from.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Púca membujuk manusia menunggang punggungnya untuk perjalanan liar lalu menjatuhkannya di tempat semula.",
        "en": "The púca entices people to ride on its back for a wild journey, then drops them back where they started."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c06",
      "source_id": "puca-s2",
      "quote": "Die bevorzugte Erscheinungsform ist die eines schwarzen Ponys.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujud kesukaannya kuda poni hitam (schwarzen Ponys).",
        "en": "Its preferred form is a black pony (schwarzen Ponys)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c07",
      "source_id": "puca-s2",
      "quote": "Bei der Ernteeinbringung ist alles, was nach Samhain auf den Feldern an Getreideähren zurückbleibt, Eigentum der Púcas. Nach Ó Súilleabháin wird Halloween auch „Púca night“ genannt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Biji-bijian di ladang setelah Samhain milik púca; menurut Ó Súilleabháin, Halloween disebut Púca night; di Irlandia (Irland). (Ireland)",
        "en": "Grain left in fields after Samhain belongs to the púcas; per Ó Súilleabháin, Halloween is called Púca night. (Ireland)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puca-c08",
      "source_id": "puca-s2",
      "quote": "Meist wirft Púca seinen „Gast“ dann eine Klippe runter oder ins Moor und verschwindet unter schallendem Gelächter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Púca biasanya menjatuhkan tamunya ke jurang atau rawa lalu menghilang sambil tertawa.",
        "en": "The púca usually throws its guest off a cliff or into a bog and vanishes laughing."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Puck, juga disebut Goodfellows atau Robin Goodfellow, adalah demon atau peri dalam cerita rakyat Inggris, berupa roh rumah tangga atau roh alam.",
    "en": "Puck, also called the Goodfellows or Robin Goodfellow, is a demon or fairy of English folklore, a household or nature sprite.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "puck-folklore-c01",
      "puck-folklore-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore), Puck, juga dikenal sebagai Goodfellows, adalah demon atau peri yang dapat berupa roh rumah tangga maupun roh alam. Asal-usul katanya tidak pasti: kata Inggris ini sudah tercatat dalam bahasa Inggris Kuno (Old English) sebagai puca, dan kata serupa muncul dalam bahasa Norse Kuno (Old Norse) púki serta bahasa Kelt seperti pwca (Wales), bucca (Cornwall), dan púca (Irlandia). Oxford English Dictionary condong ke asal Skandinavia, sedangkan Erin Sebo dari Flinders University berpendapat kata itu berasal dari Irlandia.",
      "en": "In English folklore the Puck, also known as the Goodfellows, is a demon or fairy that may be a household sprite or a nature sprite. The word's origin is unclear: it is already found in Old English as puca, and similar words appear in Old Norse púki and in Celtic tongues as pwca (Welsh), bucca (Cornish) and púca (Irish). The Oxford English Dictionary leaned to a Scandinavian source, while Erin Sebo of Flinders University argues it came from Irish.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "puck-folklore-c01",
        "puck-folklore-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Puck juga dapat disebut Goodfellows atau Hobgoblin, dengan Hob sebagai ganti Rob atau Robin, merujuk pada tokoh Robin Goodfellow. Seperti sebutan \"the good folk\" untuk peri, nama itu mencerminkan upaya menyenangkan para peri, yang suka pujian meski jahil. Rujukan tertua \"Robin Goodfellow\" menurut Oxford English Dictionary berasal dari 1531. Menurut artikel de tentang Puk, makhluk kerdil dalam legenda Nordik (nordischen Volkssagen) yang tinggal bersama manusia; bila diberi makan dan minum teratur ia membawa kemakmuran, tetapi bila diperlakukan buruk ia mendatangkan kerugian besar; artikel itu juga mengaitkannya dengan púca dari Kelt.",
      "en": "Puck may also be called the Goodfellows or Hobgoblin, Hob standing in for Rob or Robin, after the figure Robin Goodfellow. Like calling fairies 'the good folk', the name aimed to please beings fond of flattery despite their mischief. The oldest 'Robin Goodfellow' reference cited by the Oxford English Dictionary dates from 1531. The de article describes the Puk as a dwarfish figure of Nordic legend (nordischen Volkssagen) living alongside people; fed and given drink regularly it brings prosperity, but mistreated it causes grave harm; that article also links it with the Celtic púca.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "puck-folklore-c03",
        "puck-folklore-c04",
        "puck-folklore-c05",
        "puck-folklore-c06",
        "puck-folklore-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "In English folklore, The Puck (/ˈpʌk/), also known as Goodfellows, are demons or fairies which can be domestic sprites or nature sprites.",

      "id": "puck-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The present-day English word is attested already in Old English as puca (with a diminutive form pucel ).       "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini sudah ada dalam Inggris Kuno (Old English) puca; serupa Norse Kuno (Old Norse) púki, Kelt (Celtic): Wales (Welsh) pwca, Cornwall (Cornish) bucca, Irlandia (Irish) púca; Oxford English Dictionary condong Skandinavia, Erin Sebo dari Flinders University ke Irlandia. (Scandinavian)",
        "en": "Attested in Old English as puca; similar to Old Norse púki and Celtic Welsh pwca, Cornish bucca, Irish púca. (Scandinavian)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puck-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Puck may also be called The Goodfellows or Hobgoblin, in which Hob may substitute for Rob or Robin. This goes back to the character \"Robin Goodfellow\" and his name.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puck juga disebut Goodfellows atau Hobgoblin, Hob pengganti Rob atau Robin, dari Robin Goodfellow; seperti the good folk, untuk menyenangkan peri.",
        "en": "Puck is also called the Goodfellows or Hobgoblin, Hob for Rob or Robin, from Robin Goodfellow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puck-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest reference to \"Robin Goodfellow\" cited by the Oxford English Dictionary is from 1531.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rujukan tertua Robin Goodfellow menurut Oxford English Dictionary dari 1531.",
        "en": "The earliest Robin Goodfellow reference in the Oxford English Dictionary is from 1531."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puck-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "ist eine zwergenwüchsige Figur unbestimmten Geschlechts der nordischen Volkssagen. Puks leben mit den Menschen zusammen und gehören in die Gruppe der Elfen aus der germanischen Mythologie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puk sosok kerdil (zwergenwüchsige) dari legenda Nordik (nordischen Volkssagen), tinggal bersama manusia, golongan elf (Elfen) mitologi Jermanik (germanischen). Dikaitkan dengan púca Kelt (keltischen Púca).",
        "en": "The Puk is a dwarfish (zwergenwüchsige) figure of Nordic legend (nordischen Volkssagen), living with people, among the elves (Elfen) of Germanic myth. Linked with the Celtic púca (keltischen Púca)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puck-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Wird der Puk mit regelmäßigen Speisen und Getränken gut behandelt, so ist er, im Verbund mit anderen Sagengestalten wie Zwergen und Riesen, in der Lage, das Wohlergehen der Bewohner und deren Tiere positiv bis hin zum Reichtum zu beeinflussen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puk yang diberi makan-minum teratur dapat membawa kemakmuran; dikaitkan juga dengan púca Kelt.",
        "en": "A Puk given regular food and drink can bring prosperity; it is also linked with the Celtic púca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puck-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "puck-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Wird er jedoch schlecht behandelt, fügt er den Bewohnern schwere Schäden zu, die nicht selten in Wahnsinn oder mit dem Tod enden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila diperlakukan buruk, Puk mendatangkan kerugian besar, bahkan kegilaan atau kematian.",
        "en": "Mistreated, the Puk causes grave harm, even madness or death."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Baobh-shìth (\"penyihir peri\") adalah peri perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia yang muncul sebagai perempuan cantik, merayu korbannya, lalu membunuhnya.",
    "en": "The baobh-shìth ('fairy witch') is a female fairy of Scottish Highland folklore who appears as a beautiful woman, seduces her victims and then kills them.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "baobh-shith-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Baobh-shìth (harfiah \"penyihir peri\" atau \"nenek peri\" dalam bahasa Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic, jamak baobhan-sìth) adalah peri perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highlands), dengan beberapa ciri yang sama dengan succubus. Mereka muncul sebagai perempuan cantik yang merayu korban lalu menyerang dan membunuhnya. Kisah-kisahnya umumnya tentang para pemburu yang diserang di alam liar pada malam hari. Menurut artikel de, baobhan-sith adalah vampir perempuan penggoda (Vampirin) dari Skotlandia, sering berpakaian hijau, yang mengisap darah korbannya sampai tetes terakhir.",
      "en": "The baobh-shìth (Scottish Gaelic, literally 'fairy witch' or 'fairy hag', plural baobhan-sìth) is a female fairy of the Scottish Highlands sharing some traits with the succubus. They take the shape of lovely women who charm their victims, then attack and kill them. Most of their tales tell of hunters set upon in the wilds at night. According to the de article, the baobhan-sith is a seductive Scottish female vampire (Vampirin), often dressed in green, who drains her victims to the last drop of blood.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baobh-shith-c01",
        "baobh-shith-c02",
        "baobh-shith-c06",
        "baobh-shith-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam satu kisah yang dicatat Mackenzie, empat pemburu bermalam di sebuah pondok; ketika mereka menginginkan teman menari, empat perempuan masuk. Katharine Briggs menduga baobhan-sìth tidak dapat menangkap lelaki keempat di antara kuda-kuda karena besi tapal kuda, sebab besi adalah kelemahan tradisional peri. Dalam kisah serupa seorang lelaki melihat perempuan itu berkaki kuku rusa dan melarikan diri; esok paginya kawan-kawannya ditemukan dengan tenggorokan tergorok. Dalam kisah lain Macphee dilindungi anjing hitamnya yang mengusir para perempuan itu. Motif berulangnya: mereka muncul segera setelah para pemburu mengucapkan keinginan akan teman perempuan.",
      "en": "In a tale recorded by Mackenzie, four hunters shelter in a hut, and when they wish for dancing partners four women walk in. Katharine Briggs suggested the baobhan-sìth could not reach the fourth man among the horses because of the iron of their shoes, iron being a classic fairy weakness. In a similar tale one man sees that the women have deer hooves and flees; next morning his companions are found with their throats cut. In another, Macphee is saved by his black dog, which drives the women off. A recurring motif is that they arrive just after the hunters voice a wish for female company.",

        "baobh-shith-c03",
        "baobh-shith-c04",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Boggart adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Inggris, roh rumah tangga atau roh jahat penunggu suatu tempat, yang umumnya digambarkan jahat.",
    "en": "The boggart is a supernatural being of English folklore, a household spirit or malevolent spirit of a place, usually described as wicked.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "boggart-c01",
      "boggart-c02",
      "boggart-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Boggart adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore). Ahli dialek Elizabeth Wright menyebutnya \"nama umum untuk penampakan\", sedangkan folklorist Simon Young mendefinisikannya sebagai roh gaib penyendiri yang ambivalen atau jahat. Boggart ditemukan di dalam maupun di luar rumah, sebagai roh rumah tangga atau roh jahat penunggu bentang alam (genius loci). Buku Lancashire Folklore (1867) karya Harland dan Wilkinson membedakan \"house boggarts\" dari jenis lain.",
      "en": "The boggart is a supernatural being from English folklore. The dialect scholar Elizabeth Wright called it 'a generic name for an apparition', while folklorist Simon Young defines it as any lone spirit that is ambivalent or evil. Boggarts turn up both inside and outdoors, as household spirits or as malicious spirits bound to features of the landscape (a genius loci). The 1867 book Lancashire Folklore by Harland and Wilkinson separates 'house boggarts' from other kinds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "boggart-c01",
        "boggart-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Boggart umumnya digambarkan jahat: ia merangkak ke ranjang orang pada malam hari dan meletakkan tangan yang lembap dingin di wajah mereka, kadang menarik seprai, dan boggart rumah dapat mengikuti keluarga ke mana pun mereka pindah. Menurut satu sumber Lancashire, boggart tidak boleh diberi nama, karena setelah dinamai ia tak dapat lagi dibujuk dan menjadi merusak. Namanya berasal dari kata Inggris Pertengahan (Middle English) bugge, \"roh atau monster\", sekerabat dengan bogle, bugbear, dan bogeyman. Menurut artikel fr, boggart digambarkan sebagai kerdil buruk rupa, berbulu, dan jahat, kadang disebut sepupu brownie yang merosot; tapal kuda digantung di depan pintu untuk mengusirnya.",
      "en": "Boggarts are usually cast as malicious: one creeps into beds at night and lays a clammy hand on sleepers' faces, sometimes pulling off the sheets, and a house boggart may follow a family wherever they move. A Lancashire source says a boggart must never be named, since once named it can no longer be reasoned with and turns destructive. The word goes back to Middle English bugge, 'spirit or monster', and is kin to bogle, bugbear and bogeyman. According to the fr article, boggarts are pictured as ugly, hairy, spiteful dwarfs, sometimes called the brownie's degenerate cousin, and horseshoes were hung over doors to keep them away.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "boggart-c03",
        "boggart-c04",
        "boggart-c05",
        "boggart-c06",
        "boggart-c07",
        "boggart-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "boggart-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boggart",
      "title": "Boggart",
      "author": null,

    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boggart",
      "title": "Boggart",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "boggart-c01",
      "source_id": "boggart-s1",
      "quote": "A boggart is a supernatural being from English folklore. The dialectologist Elizabeth Wright described the boggart as \"a generic name for an apparition\"; folklorist Simon Young defines it as \"any ambivalent or evil solitary supernatural spirit.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boggart makhluk gaib cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore); Elizabeth Wright: nama umum penampakan; Simon Young: roh penyendiri ambivalen atau jahat; di Inggris Raya.",
        "en": "The boggart is a supernatural being of English folklore; Elizabeth Wright: a generic name for an apparition; Simon Young: any ambivalent or evil solitary spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c02",
      "source_id": "boggart-s1",
      "quote": "Boggarts are found indoors and outdoors, as a household spirit, or a malevolent spirit defined by local geography, a genius loci inhabiting topographical features. The 1867 book Lancashire Folklore by Harland and Wilkinson makes a distinction between \"house boggarts\" and other types.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boggart di dalam dan luar rumah, roh rumah atau genius loci; Lancashire Folklore (1867) Harland dan Wilkinson membedakan house boggarts.",
        "en": "Boggarts live indoors and outdoors, as household spirits or a genius loci; Lancashire Folklore (1867) by Harland and Wilkinson distinguishes house boggarts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c03",
      "source_id": "boggart-s1",
      "quote": "Typical descriptions show boggarts to be malevolent. It is said that the boggart crawls into people's beds at night and puts a clammy hand on their faces. Sometimes he strips the bedsheets off them. The household boggart may follow a family wherever they flee.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boggart jahat, merangkak ke ranjang, menaruh tangan lembap di wajah, menarik seprai, mengikuti keluarga yang pindah.",
        "en": "Boggarts are malevolent, creeping into beds, laying a clammy hand on faces, stripping sheets, following families who flee."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c04",
      "source_id": "boggart-s1",
      "quote": "One Lancashire source reports the belief that a boggart should never be named: if the boggart was given a name, it could neither be reasoned with nor persuaded, but would become uncontrollable and destructive",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber Lancashire: boggart tak boleh dinamai, atau ia jadi tak terkendali; di Yorkshire disebut boggard.",
        "en": "A Lancashire source: a boggart must never be named or it becomes uncontrollable; in Yorkshire, boggard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c05",
      "source_id": "boggart-s1",
      "quote": "These forms all point to an origin with the Middle English word bugge (a spirit or monster) with an aggrandizing suffix making boggart, a great big bugge. This also makes boggart a close linguistic cousin of \"bogle\" (or boggle, a Scots variant), 'bugaboo', \"bugbear\", \"bug\", \"bogeyman\" and \"bogie\", all of which also derive from bugge.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari Inggris Pertengahan (Middle English) bugge; sekerabat dengan bogle (Scots), bugbear, bogeyman.",
        "en": "From Middle English bugge; kin to bogle (Scots), bugbear, bogeyman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c06",
      "source_id": "boggart-s2",
      "quote": "Les boggarts (ou bwcas, bogan, bogle, boggle) sont des créatures du folklore britannique. Ils y sont décrits comme des nains hideux, velus et malveillants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boggart (bwcas, bogan, bogle) makhluk folklore Britania (britannique), kerdil buruk rupa, berbulu, jahat.",
        "en": "Boggarts (bwcas, bogan, bogle) are creatures of British (britannique) folklore, ugly, hairy, spiteful dwarfs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c07",
      "source_id": "boggart-s2",
      "quote": "Édouard Brasey prétend que le boggart serait dans l'imaginaire anglais le cousin dégénéré du brownie, un esprit servant britannique.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Édouard Brasey, boggart sepupu brownie yang merosot.",
        "en": "Per Édouard Brasey, the boggart is the brownie's degenerate cousin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boggart-c08",
      "source_id": "boggart-s2",
      "quote": "On suspendait parfois un fer à cheval devant les portes des maisons pour éloigner les boggarts.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tapal kuda digantung di depan pintu untuk mengusir boggart.",
        "en": "A horseshoe was hung before doors to keep boggarts away."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Clurichaun adalah peri jahil dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia yang sangat gemar minum dan suka menghantui tempat pembuatan bir, kedai, dan gudang anggur.",
    "en": "The clurichaun is a mischievous fairy of Irish folklore with a great love of drink, given to haunting breweries, pubs and wine cellars.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "clurichaun-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Clurichaun atau clúrachán (dari Irlandia/Irish clobhair-ceann) adalah peri jahil dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia yang terkenal gemar minum dan suka menghantui tempat pembuatan bir, kedai, serta gudang anggur. Ia berkerabat dengan leprechaun dan kadang dicampuradukkan dengannya sebagai tukang sepatu dan penjaga harta terpendam, sehingga sebagian folklorist menganggapnya leprechaun yang sedang mabuk-mabukan. Seperti leprechaun, ia peri penyendiri, bukan peri yang berkelompok. Menurut artikel de, Thomas Crofton Croker pada 1825 berpendapat kata itu berasal dari luchramán, \"kerdil\", dan kumpulan dongengnya diterjemahkan Grimm bersaudara pada 1826 sebagai Irische Elfenmärchen.",
      "en": "The clurichaun or clúrachán (from Irish clobhair-ceann) is a mischievous fairy of Irish folklore, famous for loving drink and for haunting breweries, pubs and wine cellars. He is kin to the leprechaun and is at times mixed up with him as a shoemaker and keeper of hidden treasure, leading some folklorists to see him as a leprechaun on a drinking binge. Like the leprechaun he is a solitary fairy rather than one of the trooping kind. According to the de article, Thomas Crofton Croker claimed in 1825 that the word comes from luchramán, 'dwarf', and his tales were translated by the Brothers Grimm in 1826 as Irische Elfenmärchen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "clurichaun-c01",
        "clurichaun-c02",
        "clurichaun-c06",
        "clurichaun-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam dongeng \"The Haunted Cellar\" yang dicatat Croker pada 1825, clurichaun bernama Naggeneen menghantui gudang anggur seorang bangsawan Irlandia; ia digambarkan lelaki kecil setinggi sekitar 15 cm, berwajah seperti apel layu, berhidung merah keunguan karena banyak minum, bertopi tidur merah dan bersepatu bergesper perak besar. Dalam \"Master and Man\", seorang clurichaun memaksa Billy Mac Daniel melayaninya tujuh tahun satu hari, tetapi Billy terbebas dengan menyebut berkat Tuhan. Clurichaun dapat melewati lubang kunci, mengubah rumput rawa menjadi kuda tunggangan, dan terbang menunggangi rumput itu seperti penyihir dengan sapu.",
      "en": "In 'The Haunted Cellar', recorded by Croker in 1825, a clurichaun called Naggeneen haunts an Irish lord's wine cellar; he is a little man about 15 cm tall with a face like a withered apple, a nose red and purple from drink, a red nightcap and shoes with big silver buckles. In 'Master and Man' a clurichaun forces Billy Mac Daniel to serve him for seven years and a day, but Billy breaks free by calling on God's blessing. Clurichauns can slip through keyholes, turn bog rushes into horses to ride, and fly on rushes much as witches ride brooms.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Manawydan fab Llŷr adalah tokoh mitologi Wales, putra Llŷr dan saudara Brân the Blessed serta Brânwen, yang namanya sekerabat dengan dewa laut Irlandia Manannán mac Lir.",
    "en": "Manawydan fab Llŷr is a figure of Welsh myth, son of Llŷr and brother of Brân the Blessed and Brânwen, whose name is cognate with that of the Irish sea god Manannán mac Lir.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "manawydan-fab-llyr-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Manawydan fab Llŷr adalah tokoh mitologi Wales (Welsh), putra Llŷr dan saudara Brân the Blessed serta Brânwen. Unsur pertama namanya sekerabat dengan nama dewa laut Irlandia (Irish) Manannán mac Lir dan kemungkinan berasal dari dewa Kelt (Celtic) yang sama, tetapi tidak ada bahan yang bertahan yang mengaitkannya dengan laut selain nama ayahnya (llŷr kata Wales lama untuk laut). Penampilan terpentingnya ada di Cabang Kedua dan Ketiga Mabinogi, yang terakhir dinamai menurut dia; ia juga sering disebut dalam puisi abad pertengahan dan Welsh Triads.",
      "en": "Manawydan fab Llŷr is a figure of Welsh myth, Llŷr's son and brother of Brân the Blessed and Brânwen. The first part of his name shares its root with that of the Irish sea god Manannán mac Lir and probably goes back to the same Celtic deity, yet nothing that survives ties him to the sea except his father's name (llŷr being an old Welsh word for sea). His chief appearances are in the Second and Third Branches of the Mabinogi, the latter named after him, and he is mentioned often in medieval verse and the Welsh Triads.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c01",
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Cabang Kedua ia menjadi penasihat saudaranya, Brân the Blessed, raja Britania (Britain), dan termasuk tujuh orang yang selamat dari perang di Irlandia; ia ikut membawa kepala Brân yang terus berbicara. Dalam Cabang Ketiga, Pryderi mengajaknya tinggal di Dyfed dan menikahi Rhiannon, ibu Pryderi; kemudian sebuah mantra membuat semua orang dan hewan ternak di Dyfed lenyap. Menurut artikel de, ia digambarkan dalam Mabinogion sebagai cendekiawan dan penyihir yang cinta damai, dan seperti senamanya di Irlandia ia juga dipandang sebagai dewa laut; menurut Birkhan namanya berasal dari Manaw, nama Wales untuk Isle of Man.",
      "en": "In the Second Branch he advises his brother Brân the Blessed, king of Britain, and is one of seven men to survive the war in Ireland, helping carry Brân's still-speaking head. In the Third Branch Pryderi invites him to live in Dyfed and marry Rhiannon, Pryderi's mother; then a spell makes every person and farm animal in Dyfed vanish. According to the de article the Mabinogion shows him as a peaceable scholar and magician, and like his Irish namesake he is also seen as a sea god; per Birkhan his name comes from Manaw, the Welsh name of the Isle of Man.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c03",
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c04",
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c05",
        "manawydan-fab-llyr-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Afanc adalah monster danau dari mitologi Wales yang wujudnya digambarkan beragam, seperti buaya, berang-berang, atau makhluk mirip kerdil.",
    "en": "The afanc is a lake monster of Welsh myth variously pictured as like a crocodile, a beaver or a dwarf-like creature.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "afanc-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Afanc (kadang disebut Addanc) adalah monster danau dari mitologi Wales (Welsh). Gambarannya berbeda-beda: mirip buaya, berang-berang, makhluk seperti kerdil, atau platipus, dan kadang dikatakan iblis. Danau tempat tinggalnya pun tak pasti; ia disebut tinggal di Llyn Llion, Llyn Barfog, dekat Brynberian Bridge, atau di Llyn yr Afanc, kolam di Afon Conwy dekat Betws-y-coed yang dinamai menurut makhluk itu. Seperti kebanyakan monster danau, ia memangsa siapa pun yang cukup bodoh untuk jatuh atau berenang di danaunya. Menurut artikel de, kulitnya sangat kuat sehingga sulit, bahkan mustahil, dilukai dengan senjata biasa.",
      "en": "The afanc, sometimes called the Addanc, is a lake monster from Welsh myth. Accounts differ: it resembles a crocodile, a beaver, a dwarfish being or a platypus, and is sometimes called a demon. Where it lives is just as unsettled: Llyn Llion and Llyn Barfog are named, as is a spot by Brynberian Bridge, and so is Llyn yr Afanc, a pool on the Afon Conwy close to Betws-y-coed that takes its name from the beast. Like most lake monsters it preys on anyone foolish enough to fall or swim into its water. According to the de article its hide is so tough that ordinary weapons can hardly if ever wound it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "afanc-c01",
        "afanc-c02",
        "afanc-c03",
        "afanc-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel de, dalam satu kisah seorang gadis membiarkan Addanc tidur di pangkuannya sementara penduduk desa merantainya; ketika terbangun dan mengamuk, ia meremukkan gadis itu. Akhirnya ia diseret ke danau Cwm Ffynnon atau dibunuh Peredur, dan legenda Britania kemudian memberi jasa itu kepada Raja Arthur atau Parzival. Dalam versi Edward Williams, Addanc ditarik keluar dari Llyn Llion oleh lembu-lembu Hu Gadarn dan mudah dibunuh begitu tidak lagi berada di air. Bedd yr Afanc (\"Makam Monster\"), satu-satunya makam galeri di Wales, dikaitkan dengan makhluk ini.",
      "en": "According to the de article, in one tale a maiden let the Addanc sleep in her lap while villagers chained it; waking in rage, it crushed her. In the end it was dragged off to the lake Cwm Ffynnon or slain by Peredur, and later British legend gave the deed to King Arthur or Parzival. In Edward Williams's version, Hu Gadarn's oxen hauled the Addanc out of Llyn Llion and it was easily killed once out of the water. Bedd yr Afanc ('Grave of the Monster'), the only gallery grave in Wales, is linked with the creature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "afanc-c06",
        "afanc-c07",
        "afanc-c08",
        "afanc-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The lake in which it dwells is also uncertain and it is variously said to live in Llyn Llion, Llyn Barfog, near Brynberian Bridge or in Llyn yr Afanc, a pool on the Afon Conwy near Betws-y-coed that was named after the creature.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tempatnya Llyn Llion, Llyn Barfog, dekat Brynberian Bridge, atau Llyn yr Afanc di Afon Conwy dekat Betws-y-coed.",
        "en": "It lives in Llyn Llion, Llyn Barfog, near Brynberian Bridge or Llyn yr Afanc on the Afon Conwy near Betws-y-coed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c03",
      "source_id": "afanc-s1",
      "quote": "The afanc was a monstrous creature that, like most lake monsters, was said to prey upon any foolish enough to fall into or swim in its lake.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Afanc memangsa siapa pun yang jatuh atau berenang di danaunya.",
        "en": "The afanc preys on anyone falling or swimming in its lake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c04",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Es existieren mehrere Synonyme für das Wesen, wie etwa Afanc [ ˈavaŋk] (wohl das gebräuchlichste), Addane, Adanc, Avanc, Abhac und Abac.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sinonimnya Afanc, Addane, Adanc, Avanc, Abhac, Abac.",
        "en": "Synonyms are Afanc, Addane, Adanc, Avanc, Abhac, Abac."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c05",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Es besitzt eine sehr starke Haut, was es sehr schwer, wenn nicht gar unmöglich macht, es mit herkömmlichen Waffen zu verletzen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kulitnya sangat kuat (sehr starke Haut), sulit dilukai senjata biasa.",
        "en": "Its very tough hide (sehr starke Haut) is hard to wound with ordinary weapons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c06",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Von einer anderen Sage wird erzählt, in der eine Jungfrau es bezwang, indem sie es in ihrem Schoß schlafen ließ; sogleich eilten die Dorfbewohner herbei und legten das Untier in Ketten. Als es erwachte, geriet es in Zorn und zerschmetterte rasend das Mädchen, in dessen Schoß es noch immer lag.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seorang gadis (Jungfrau) membiarkannya tidur di pangkuan, penduduk desa merantainya; saat bangun ia meremukkan gadis itu.",
        "en": "A maiden (Jungfrau) let it sleep in her lap and villagers chained it; on waking it crushed her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c07",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Am Ende wurde es entweder in den See Cwm Ffynnon gezogen oder aber von Peredur getötet. In späteren britischen Legenden wird die Tötung der Kreatur entweder König Artus oder Parzival (der Name Peredurs in der Artussage) zugesprochen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akhirnya diseret ke danau Cwm Ffynnon atau dibunuh Peredur; legenda kemudian menyebut Raja Arthur (König Artus) atau Parzival. (British, britischen)",
        "en": "It was dragged to Cwm Ffynnon or killed by Peredur; later legends credit King Arthur (König Artus) or Parzival. (British, britischen)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c08",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Einer anderen Fassung von Williams zufolge wurde das Addanc von den Ochsen des Hu Gadarn aus dem See in Llyn Llion gezogen, und konnte, sobald es nicht mehr im Wasser schwamm, leicht getötet werden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Edward Williams, Addanc ditarik keluar dari Llyn Llion oleh lembu Hu Gadarn dan mudah dibunuh di darat.",
        "en": "Per Edward Williams the Addanc was dragged from Llyn Llion by Hu Gadarn's oxen and easily killed on land."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "afanc-c09",
      "source_id": "afanc-s2",
      "quote": "Bedd yr Afanc („Das Grab des Ungeheuers“) ist das einzige in Wales zu findende Galeriegrab und wird mit Addanc verbunden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bedd yr Afanc (Makam Monster, Grab des Ungeheuers), satu-satunya makam galeri (Galeriegrab) di Wales, dikaitkan dengan Addanc.",
        "en": "Bedd yr Afanc (Grave of the Monster, Grab des Ungeheuers), the only gallery grave (Galeriegrab) in Wales, is linked to the Addanc."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Black Annis adalah sosok momok perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Inggris, nenek sihir berwajah biru dengan cakar besi yang memakan anak-anak, tinggal di gua di Dane Hills, Leicestershire.",
    "en": "Black Annis is a bogeywoman of English folklore, a blue-faced hag with iron claws who eats children, living in a cave in the Dane Hills of Leicestershire.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "black-annis-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Black Annis (juga Black Agnes atau Black Anna) adalah sosok momok perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore). Ia dibayangkan sebagai nenek sihir berwajah biru dengan cakar besi dan doyan daging manusia, terutama anak-anak. Ia dikatakan menghantui pedesaan Leicestershire dan tinggal di gua di Dane Hills dengan pohon ek besar di pintu masuknya. Pada malam hari ia keluar mencari anak-anak dan anak domba untuk dimakan, lalu menyamak kulit mereka dengan menggantungnya di pohon sebelum memakainya di pinggang.",
      "en": "Black Annis (also called Black Agnes or Black Anna) is a bogeywoman of English folklore. People pictured her as a blue-faced hag with iron claws and a hunger for human flesh, children above all. She was said to haunt the Leicestershire countryside from a cave in the Dane Hills with a great oak at its mouth. At night she went hunting for children and lambs to eat, then tanned their skins on a tree and wore them around her waist.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "black-annis-c01",
        "black-annis-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Konon pondok-pondok di Leicestershire sengaja dibangun berjendela kecil agar Black Annis hanya bisa memasukkan satu lengan; lolongannya terdengar sejauh 8 km. Rujukan tertulis tertua tentangnya berasal dari akta tanah abad ke-18 yang menyebut sebidang tanah \"Black Anny's Bower Close\"; County Folklore (1895) menyebut dua akta bertanggal 1764. T. C. Lethbridge mengaitkan asal-usulnya dengan Danu (Anu) dari mitologi Kelt, tetapi Ronald Hutton tidak sependapat dengan teori-teori semacam itu. Menurut artikel de, ia juga pernah terlihat sebagai kucing dan digunakan orang tua untuk menakuti anak-anak yang tidak patuh.",
      "en": "Leicestershire cottages were reportedly built with small windows so Black Annis could reach in with only one arm, and her howl carried 8 km. The oldest written mention is an 18th-century title deed naming a plot 'Black Anny's Bower Close'; County Folklore (1895) cites two such deeds dated 1764. T. C. Lethbridge traced her to the Celtic Danu (Anu), though Ronald Hutton rejects such theories. According to the de article she was also seen in the form of a cat and was used by parents to frighten disobedient children.",

      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "Black Annis (also known as Black Agnes or Black Anna) is a bogeywoman figure in English folklore. She is imagined as a blue-faced hag or witch with iron claws and a taste for human flesh (especially children). She is said to haunt the countryside of Leicestershire, living in a cave in the Dane Hills with a great oak tree at the entrance.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Black Annis (Black Agnes, Black Anna) momok perempuan cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore), nenek sihir berwajah biru bercakar besi pemakan anak, di gua Dane Hills, Leicestershire; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "Black Annis (Black Agnes, Black Anna) is a bogeywoman of English folklore, a blue-faced iron-clawed hag eating children, in a Dane Hills cave in Leicestershire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c02",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "She is said to venture out at night looking for unsuspecting children and lambs to eat, then tanning their skins by hanging them on a tree before wearing them around her waist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keluar malam mencari anak dan anak domba, menyamak kulitnya, memakainya di pinggang.",
        "en": "She goes out at night for children and lambs, tans their skins, wears them at her waist."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c03",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that cottages in Leicestershire were purposely built with small windows so that Black Annis could only get a single arm inside. When she howled, she could be heard 5 mi (8.0 km) away",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pondok Leicestershire berjendela kecil; lolongannya terdengar 8 km.",
        "en": "Leicestershire cottages had small windows; her howl was heard 8 km away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c04",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest known written reference to Black Annis was from an eighteenth century title deed that referred to a parcel of land (or \"close\") as \"Black Anny's Bower Close\". The first volume of County Folklore (1895), published by The Folklore Society, mentions two such title deeds dated May 13th and 14th, 1764.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rujukan tertua: akta tanah abad ke-18, Black Anny's Bower Close; County Folklore (1895) menyebut dua akta 1764.",
        "en": "Earliest reference: an 18th-century deed, Black Anny's Bower Close; County Folklore (1895) cites two 1764 deeds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c05",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "Some have claimed, as T. C. Lethbridge did, that the origin can be found in Celtic mythology based on Danu (or Anu) or it may derive from Germanic mythology (see Hel).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "T. C. Lethbridge: asal dari mitologi Kelt (Celtic), Danu (Anu).",

    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c06",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s1",
      "quote": "Ronald Hutton however disagrees with such theories in his book The Triumph of the Moon",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ronald Hutton tidak sependapat dengan teori itu.",

    },
    {
      "id": "black-annis-c07",
      "source_id": "black-annis-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Blue men of the Minch adalah makhluk mitologi Skotlandia yang menghuni selat antara Outer Hebrides utara dan daratan Skotlandia, menenggelamkan pelaut dan kapal.",
    "en": "The blue men of the Minch are Scottish mythological beings of the strait between the northern Outer Hebrides and mainland Scotland, who drown sailors and sink ships.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "blue-men-of-the-minch-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Blue men of the Minch, juga disebut storm kelpies (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic: na fir ghorma), adalah makhluk mitologi yang menghuni perairan antara Outer Hebrides utara dan daratan Skotlandia (Scotland), mencari pelaut untuk ditenggelamkan dan kapal untuk dikaramkan. Selain warnanya yang biru, mereka mirip manusia dan seukuran manusia. Mereka dapat menciptakan badai, tetapi saat cuaca cerah mereka mengapung tidur di permukaan air. Mereka berenang dengan tubuh bagian atas terangkat dari laut, berputar dan menyelam seperti lumba-lumba.",
      "en": "The blue men of the Minch, also called storm kelpies (Scottish Gaelic na fir ghorma), are mythical beings of the waters between the northern Outer Hebrides and mainland Scotland who seek sailors to drown and boats to wreck. Blue in colour but otherwise human-like, they are about human size. They can raise storms, but in fair weather they drift asleep at or just under the surface. They swim with their upper bodies out of the sea, turning and diving like porpoises.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "blue-men-of-the-minch-c01",
        "blue-men-of-the-minch-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka dapat berbicara; ketika sekelompok mendekati kapal, pemimpinnya kadang meneriakkan dua baris puisi kepada nakhoda dan menantangnya melengkapi bait itu, dan jika gagal mereka berusaha membalikkan kapalnya. Menurut sebagian penjelasan, mereka mungkin personifikasi laut, berasal dari bangsa Pict yang bertubuh dicat, atau dari budak Afrika Utara yang dibawa bangsa Viking ke Skotlandia. Menurut Donald A. Mackenzie, mereka tidak punya padanan di tempat lain, bahkan di wilayah Skotlandia lain. Menurut artikel es, mereka mungkin bagian dari suku \"malaikat jatuh\" yang terpecah tiga: peri darat, orang biru laut, dan \"penari riang\" Aurora di langit.",
      "en": "They can talk; when a group nears a ship its chief may shout two lines of verse to the skipper and dare him to finish the rhyme, and if he fails they try to overturn the vessel. Explanations suggest they personify the sea, or come from the painted Picts, or from North African slaves brought to Scotland by the Vikings. According to Donald A. Mackenzie they have no counterpart anywhere else, not even elsewhere in Scotland. According to the es article they may belong to a tribe of 'fallen angels' split in three: land fairies, the sea's blue men, and the 'merry dancers' of the Aurora in the sky.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c01",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s1",
      "quote": "The blue men of the Minch, also known as storm kelpies (Scottish Gaelic: na fir ghorma Scottish Gaelic pronunciation: [nə fiɾʲ ˈɣɔɾɔmə] ), are mythological creatures inhabiting the stretch of water between the northern Outer Hebrides and mainland Scotland, looking for sailors to drown and stricken boats to sink.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Blue men of the Minch (storm kelpies, Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic na fir ghorma) makhluk mitologi perairan antara Outer Hebrides utara dan daratan Skotlandia (Scotland), menenggelamkan pelaut; di Britania Raya. (Kelpie)",
        "en": "The blue men of the Minch (storm kelpies, Scottish Gaelic na fir ghorma) inhabit the waters between the northern Outer Hebrides and mainland Scotland, drowning sailors. (Kelpie)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c02",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s1",
      "quote": "Apart from their blue colour, the mythical creatures look much like humans, and are about the same size. They have the power to create storms, but when the weather is fine they float sleeping on or just below the surface of the water.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Biru tapi mirip manusia, seukuran manusia; menciptakan badai; tidur mengapung saat cerah; berenang seperti lumba-lumba.",
        "en": "Blue but humanlike, human-sized; they create storms and float asleep in fair weather."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c03",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s1",
      "quote": "They are able to speak, and when a group approaches a ship its chief may shout two lines of poetry to the master of the vessel and challenge him to complete the verse. If the skipper fails in that task then the blue men will attempt to capsize his ship.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemimpinnya meneriakkan dua baris puisi; jika nakhoda gagal melengkapinya, kapal dibalikkan.",
        "en": "Their chief shouts two lines of verse; if the skipper fails to complete it, they capsize the ship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c04",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s1",
      "quote": "Suggestions to explain the mythical blue men include that they may be a personification of the sea, or originate with the Picts, whose painted bodies may have given the impression of men raising themselves out of the water",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjelasan: personifikasi laut, atau bangsa Pict (Picts) bertubuh dicat; juga budak Afrika Utara (North African) yang dibawa Viking (Vikings) ke Skotlandia.",
        "en": "Explanations: a personification of the sea, or the painted Picts; also North African slaves brought by the Vikings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c05",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s1",
      "quote": "According to Donald A. Mackenzie they have no counterparts elsewhere in the world or even in other areas of Scotland",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Donald A. Mackenzie tak ada padanannya di tempat lain di Skotlandia (Scotland).",
        "en": "Per Donald A. Mackenzie they have no counterparts elsewhere, even in Scotland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c06",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s2",
      "quote": "Los hombres azules podrían haber sido parte de una tribu de \"ángeles caídos\" que se dividieron en tres; la primera se convirtió en las hadas de la tierra, la segunda se convirtió en los hombres azules del mar, y los restantes se convirtieron en los \"bailarines felices\" de la Aurora polar en el cielo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mungkin bagian suku malaikat jatuh (ángeles caídos) yang terpecah tiga: peri darat, orang biru laut, penari riang (bailarines felices) Aurora di langit.",
        "en": "Perhaps from a tribe of fallen angels (ángeles caídos) split in three: land fairies, sea blue men, the merry dancers (bailarines felices) of the Aurora."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-c07",
      "source_id": "blue-men-of-the-minch-s2",
      "quote": "Se ha sugerido que los hombres azules míticos puedan ser una personificación del mar, o que tienen su origen en los pictos",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang biru (hombres azules) mungkin personifikasi laut (mar) atau berasal dari Pict (pictos).",
        "en": "The blue men (hombres azules) may personify the sea (mar) or come from the Picts (pictos)."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Dea Matrona (\"Ibu Ilahi\") adalah dewi Kelt yang memberi nama Sungai Marne (Matrona kuno) di Galia dan ditafsirkan sebagai dewi ibu.",
    "en": "Dea Matrona ('Divine Mother') is the Celtic goddess who gave her name to the river Marne (ancient Matrona) in Gaul, read as a mother goddess.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dea-matrona-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Kelt (Celtic), Dea Matrona (\"Ibu Ilahi\") adalah dewi yang memberi nama Sungai Marne (Matrŏna kuno) di Galia (Gaul). Nama dewa Galia (Gaulish) Mātr-on-ā berarti \"Ibu Agung\", dan dewi Marne ditafsirkan sebagai dewi ibu. Banyak gambar keagamaan Galia, termasuk patung terakota murah untuk altar rumah, menggambarkan dewi ibu yang menyusui bayi atau memangku buah, makanan, atau anjing kecil; di banyak daerah Matronae seperti itu digambarkan bertiga atau berdua. Nama tokoh mitologi Wales (Welsh) Modron, ibu Mabon, berasal dari akar kata yang sama.",
      "en": "In Celtic myth Dea Matrona ('Divine Mother') is the goddess whose name the river Marne (ancient Matrŏna) in Gaul bears. The Gaulish divine name Mātr-on-ā means 'Great Mother', and the Marne's goddess is taken to be a mother goddess. Many Gaulish sacred images, among them cheap terracotta figures made in bulk for home shrines, show mother goddesses suckling infants or holding fruit, food or little dogs in their laps; in many regions such Matronae appear in threes or pairs. The Welsh mythological figure Modron, mother of Mabon, takes her name from the same root.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dea-matrona-c01",
        "dea-matrona-c02",
        "dea-matrona-c03",
        "dea-matrona-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel de, Matrona adalah dewi sungai yang dipahami sebagai personifikasi Sungai Marne. Pemujaannya dibuktikan oleh sebuah prasasti persembahan yang ditemukan pada 1831 di daerah hulu sungai itu (\"La Marnotte\") dekat Bellesmes, Haute-Marne, tak jauh dari Langres. Gaius Iulius Caesar dalam De bello Gallico menyebut Matrona sebagai nama Sungai Marne, yang bersama Sequana (Seine) menjadi batas antara Galia dan Belgica. Nama Matrona berasal dari kata Galia mātīr (\"ibu\").",
      "en": "According to the de article, Matrona is a river goddess understood as the personification of the Marne. Her cult is attested by a votive inscription found in 1831 at the river's source ('La Marnotte') near Bellesmes in Haute-Marne, not far from Langres. Gaius Iulius Caesar, in De bello Gallico, gives Matrona as the name of the Marne, which with the Sequana (Seine) bordered Gaul and Belgica. The name comes from Gaulish mātīr, 'mother'.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dobhar-chú (\"anjing air\") atau King otter adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Irlandia dan Skotlandia yang mirip anjing dan berang-berang serta hidup di air.",
    "en": "Known as the Dobhar-chú ('water hound') or King otter, this dog- and otter-like being of Irish and Scottish lore lives in water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dobhar-chu-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dobhar-chú (harfiah \"anjing air\"), atau King otter, adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish) dan Skotlandia (Scottish) yang mirip anjing sekaligus berang-berang, meski kadang digambarkan setengah anjing setengah ikan. Bulunya dikatakan bersifat melindungi dan ia hidup di air. Legendanya hampir sepenuhnya bergantung pada tradisi lisan. Dobharchú adalah salah satu dari dua kata Irlandia paling umum untuk \"berang-berang\"; dobhar bentuk kuno untuk air dan cú berarti anjing pemburu. Ia digambarkan mirip berang-berang tetapi sekitar lima kali lebih besar, 3–4,6 m, berbulu putih dengan ujung telinga hitam dan tanda seperti salib hitam di punggung.",
      "en": "The Dobhar-chú (literally 'water hound'), or King otter, is a being of Irish and Scottish folklore that looks like both a dog and an otter, though it is sometimes called half dog, half fish. Its fur is said to have protective powers and it dwells in water. Its legend rests almost entirely on oral tradition. Dobharchú is one of the two commonest Irish words for 'otter'; dobhar is an old word for water and cú means hound. It is pictured as an otter about five times normal size, 3–4.6 m long, with a white pelt, black ear tips and a black cross-like mark on its back.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dobhar-chu-c01",
        "dobhar-chu-c02",
        "dobhar-chu-c03",
        "dobhar-chu-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah batu nisan di pemakaman Congbháil (Conwall) di Glenade, County Leitrim, menggambarkan Dobhar-chú dan dikaitkan dengan kisah serangan makhluk itu terhadap seorang perempuan setempat pada abad kedelapan belas. Menurut legenda, pada 1722 Grace McGloighlin pergi ke danau Glenade untuk mencuci pakaian; suaminya, Terrence, menemukan jasadnya yang tercabik dengan Dobhar-chú tidur di atasnya, lalu membunuhnya dengan belati. Saat sekarat makhluk itu bersiul memanggil pasangannya, yang mengejar Terrence hingga ia juga membunuhnya setelah pertarungan panjang. Menurut artikel es, nama perempuan itu disebut Gráinne dan peristiwanya terjadi pada abad ke-17.",
      "en": "A headstone in Congbháil (Conwall) Cemetery at Glenade, County Leitrim, shows the Dobhar-chú and is tied to a tale of the creature killing a local woman in the eighteenth century. In the legend, in 1722 Grace McGloighlin went down to Glenade Lough to wash clothes; her husband Terrence found her mangled body with the Dobhar-chú asleep on it and killed it with a dagger. Dying, it gave a whistling cry to its mate, which chased Terrence until he killed it too after a long fight. According to the es article the woman was named Gráinne and the event took place in the 17th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dobhar-chu-c05",
        "dobhar-chu-c06",
        "dobhar-chu-c07",
        "dobhar-chu-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fairy Queen atau Ratu Peri adalah penguasa perempuan para peri dalam cerita rakyat dan sastra, kadang berpasangan dengan raja; Titania dan Mab adalah nama yang sering dipakai.",
    "en": "In folk tradition and in literature the Fairy Queen, or Queen of the Fairies, rules over the fairies, sometimes alongside a king; Titania and Mab are common names.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fairy-queen-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat dan sastra, Fairy Queen atau Queen of the Fairies adalah penguasa perempuan para peri, kadang tetapi tidak selalu berpasangan dengan seorang raja. Tergantung karyanya, ia bisa bernama atau tanpa nama; Titania dan Mab adalah dua nama yang sering dipakai. Tuatha Dé Danann dan Daoine Sidhe dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish) memiliki banyak raja dan ratu setempat; Oonagh (Una, Nuala) adalah istri Finvarra, raja peri Irlandia (Ireland) barat. Ratu peri Irlandia lainnya termasuk Clíodhna dari Munster, Aibhell, dan Ainé.",
      "en": "Folk tradition and literature know a Fairy Queen, also called Queen of the Fairies, a woman ruling over the fairies, at times though not always beside a king. Some works name her and some do not; Titania and Mab are two names used often. The Tuatha Dé Danann and Daoine Sidhe of Irish myth had many local kings and queens; Oonagh (Una, Nuala) was wife of Finvarra, fairy king of the west of Ireland. Other Irish fairy queens include Clíodhna of Munster, Aibhell and Ainé.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fairy-queen-c01",
        "fairy-queen-c02",
        "fairy-queen-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tokoh Arthurian Morgan le Fey memerintah pulau gaib Avalon dan kadang digambarkan sebagai ratu peri; dalam Vita Merlini ia Morgen, kepala sembilan ratu bersaudara yang sakti. Ratu peri muncul dalam beberapa Child Ballads: yang baik hati dalam Alison Gross, dan yang kejam dalam Tam Lin, yang membayar upeti kepada Neraka setiap tujuh tahun. Dalam Thomas the Rhymer, ratu peri tanpa nama menjadikan Thomas kekasihnya dan meninggalkannya dengan kemampuan bernubuat. Menurut artikel es, Child Ballads adalah kumpulan 305 balada rakyat dari Inggris (Inglaterra) dan Skotlandia (Escocia) yang dihimpun Francis James Child.",
      "en": "The Arthurian Morgan le Fey ruled the otherworldly island of Avalon and was at times shown as a fairy queen; in the Vita Merlini she is Morgen, head of nine magical sister queens. Fairy queens appear in some Child Ballads: a kindly one in Alison Gross and a cruel one in Tam Lin, who pays a tithe to Hell every seven years. In Thomas the Rhymer an unnamed fairy queen takes Thomas as her lover and leaves him able to prophesy. According to the es article the Child Ballads are a set of 305 folk ballads from England (Inglaterra) and Scotland (Escocia) gathered by Francis James Child.",

      "id": "fairy-queen-c02",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s1",
      "quote": "The Tuatha Dé Danann and the Daoine Sidhe of Irish mythology had numerous local kings and queens. Oonagh, Una or Nuala was the wife of Finvarra or Fionnbharr, the fairy king of western Ireland",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tuatha Dé Danann dan Daoine Sidhe mitologi Irlandia (Irish) punya banyak raja-ratu; Oonagh (Una, Nuala) istri Finvarra, raja peri Irlandia (Ireland) barat.",
        "en": "The Tuatha Dé Danann and Daoine Sidhe of Irish myth had many kings and queens; Oonagh (Una, Nuala) was wife of Finvarra, fairy king of western Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fairy-queen-c03",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s1",
      "quote": "Other Irish fairy queens included Clíodhna of Munster, Aibhell and Ainé.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ratu peri Irlandia lain: Clíodhna dari Munster, Aibhell, Ainé.",
        "en": "Other Irish fairy queens: Clíodhna of Munster, Aibhell, Ainé."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fairy-queen-c04",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s1",
      "quote": "The Arthurian character of Morgan le Fey (or Morgan of the Fairies) ruled the supernatural island of Avalon and was sometimes depicted as a fairy queen. In the Vita Merlini , she is Morgen, the chief of nine magical sister queens.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Morgan le Fey (Arthurian) memerintah Avalon, kadang ratu peri; dalam Vita Merlini, Morgen kepala sembilan ratu.",
        "en": "Arthurian Morgan le Fey ruled Avalon and was sometimes a fairy queen; in the Vita Merlini, Morgen heads nine queens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fairy-queen-c05",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s1",
      "quote": "A kind and helpful fairy queen features in Alison Gross (Child 35), and a terrible and deadly fairy queen is the antagonist of Tam Lin (Child 39). Tam Lin's Fairy Queen pays a tithe to Hell every seven years",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ratu peri baik dalam Alison Gross, kejam dalam Tam Lin yang membayar upeti ke Neraka (Hell) tiap tujuh tahun; Child Ballads.",
        "en": "A kind fairy queen in Alison Gross, a deadly one in Tam Lin who pays a tithe to Hell every seven years; Child Ballads."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fairy-queen-c06",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s1",
      "quote": "An unnamed fairy queen appears in Thomas the Rhymer (Child 37), where she takes the titular character as her lover and leaves him with prophetic abilities.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Thomas the Rhymer ratu peri menjadikan Thomas kekasih dan memberinya kemampuan bernubuat.",
        "en": "In Thomas the Rhymer a fairy queen takes Thomas as lover and gives him prophetic powers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fairy-queen-c07",
      "source_id": "fairy-queen-s2",
      "quote": "Las reinas de las hadas aparecen en algunas de las Baladas Infantiles, un conjunto de 305 baladas populares de Inglaterra y Escocia recopiladas por Francis James Child.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Reina de las hadas muncul dalam Child Ballads, 305 balada rakyat Inggris (Inglaterra) dan Skotlandia (Escocia) himpunan Francis James Child.",
        "en": "Fairy queens (reinas de las hadas) appear in the Child Ballads, 305 folk ballads of England (Inglaterra) and Scotland (Escocia) collected by Francis James Child."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fand adalah perempuan dunia lain dalam mitologi Irlandia, istri Manannán, yang menjadi kekasih Cúchulainn dalam kisah Serglige Con Culainn.",
    "en": "Fand is an otherworldly woman of Irish myth, wife of Manannán, who becomes Cúchulainn's lover in the tale Serglige Con Culainn.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fand-c01",
      "fand-c02",
      "fand-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fand (\"air mata\", \"tetes air mata keindahan\") atau Fann (\"orang lemah, tak berdaya\") adalah perempuan dunia lain dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish). Kedua bentuk namanya bukan varian bunyi, melainkan dua kata berbeda makna, dan sejarah namanya masih diperdebatkan. Ia paling menonjol dalam kisah Siklus Ulster (Ulster Cycle) Serglige Con Culainn (\"The Sickbed of Cúchulainn\") sebagai putri Áed Abrat, saudari Lí Ban, dan istri Manannán. Menurut artikel de, ia dewi laut (Meeresgöttin) sekaligus ratu para elf dan disebut dewi paling cantik.",
      "en": "An otherworldly woman of Irish myth, she is called Fand, meaning 'tear' or 'teardrop of beauty', or Fann, meaning 'weak, helpless person'. Her two names are not sound variants but separate words with separate meanings, and their history is argued over. Her biggest role comes in Serglige Con Culainn ('The Sickbed of Cúchulainn'), a story of the Ulster Cycle, where she is Áed Abrat's daughter, Lí Ban's sister and Manannán's wife. According to the de article she is a sea goddess (Meeresgöttin) and also a queen of the elves, said to be the loveliest of goddesses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fand-c01",
        "fand-c02",
        "fand-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia masuk ke cerita dalam wujud burung laut dunia lain, terbang bersama Lí Ban dengan rantai emas. Cúchulainn melempar batu yang menembus bulu sayapnya; kemudian Fand dan Lí Ban kembali sebagai perempuan dan mencambuknya hingga ia sakit setahun. Ia sembuh berkat Fand setelah setuju membantunya berperang di pulau dunia lainnya, dan keduanya menjadi kekasih. Namun Emer, istri Cúchulainn, cemburu dan menyerang dengan sekelompok perempuan bersenjata pisau; Fand memilih pergi dan kembali kepada Manannán, yang mengibaskan jubah kabutnya di antara Fand dan Cúchulainn agar mereka tak bertemu lagi.",
      "en": "She enters the story as an otherworldly seabird, flying with Lí Ban joined by a gold chain. Cúchulainn hurls a stone that passes through her wing feathers; later Fand and Lí Ban come back as women and whip him until he lies sick for a year. He recovers through Fand's favour after agreeing to fight for her on her otherworld island, and they become lovers. But Cúchulainn's wife Emer, jealous, attacks with a band of knife-armed women; Fand chooses to leave and returns to Manannán, who shakes his cloak of mists between Fand and Cúchulainn so they may never meet again.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fand-c03",

      ]

      "quote": "Fand (\"tear\", \"teardrop of beauty\") or Fann (\"weak, helpless person'\") is an otherworldly woman in Irish mythology. The two forms of her name are not phonetic variants, but two different words of different meaning and the history of her name is debated.",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "fand-c02",
      "source_id": "fand-s1",
      "quote": "Fand appears most prominently in the Ulster Cycle tale, Serglige Con Culainn (\"The Sickbed of Cúchulainn\") as the daughter of Áed Abrat, sister of Lí Ban and one Angus, and wife of Manannán.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Serglige Con Culainn (Siklus Ulster, Ulster Cycle), putri Áed Abrat, saudari Lí Ban, istri Manannán (Manannán mac Lir).",
        "en": "In Serglige Con Culainn (Ulster Cycle), daughter of Áed Abrat, sister of Lí Ban, wife of Manannán (Manannán mac Lir)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fand-c03",
      "source_id": "fand-s1",
      "quote": "She enters the story in the form of an otherworldly sea bird. In her sea bird form, she flies with a flock of enchanted birds, with each pair joined together by a silver chain. Fand, flying with her sister Lí Ban, stands out from the rest as they are connected by a gold chain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fand berwujud burung laut dunia lain, terbang bersama Lí Ban dengan rantai emas.",
        "en": "Fand appears as an otherworldly seabird, flying with Lí Ban joined by a gold chain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fand-c04",
      "source_id": "fand-s1",
      "quote": "Later, Fand and Lí Ban return in the form of \"Otherworldly women\" and confront him on the shore of the lake. They beat Cúchulainn with horsewhips until he falls ill and lies abed for a year, unable to rise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka mencambuk Cúchulainn hingga ia sakit setahun; kemudian Cúchulainn dan Fand menjadi kekasih.",
        "en": "They whip Cúchulainn until he lies sick a year; later Cúchulainn and Fand become lovers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fand-c05",
      "source_id": "fand-s1",
      "quote": "She chants a poem, and then returns to her husband Manannán, who shakes his magical cloak of mists between Fand and Cúchulainn, that they may never meet again.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Emer cemburu; Fand kembali kepada Manannán yang mengibaskan jubah kabut di antara Fand dan Cúchulainn.",
        "en": "Emer is jealous; Fand returns to Manannán, who shakes his cloak of mists between Fand and Cúchulainn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fand-c06",
      "source_id": "fand-s2",
      "quote": "Sie gilt als Tochter Aed Abraths von den Túatha Dé Danann, ist eine Meeresgöttin, aber auch Königin der Elfen und soll die schönste aller Göttinnen sein.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fand putri Aed Abrath dari Túatha Dé Danann, dewi laut (Meeresgöttin), ratu elf (Königin der Elfen), dewi tercantik.",
        "en": "Fand is daughter of Aed Abrath of the Túatha Dé Danann, a sea goddess (Meeresgöttin), queen of the elves (Königin der Elfen), loveliest of goddesses."

    {

      "claim_ids": [
        "grannus-c01",
        "grannus-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "disposition": {
    "value": "benevolent",
    "claim_ids": [
      "grannus-c01"
    ]
  },
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Grannus adalah dewa penyembuhan Kelt kuno yang dikaitkan dengan mata air dan air penyembuh, pada masa Romawi disamakan dengan Apollo dan berpasangan dengan dewi Sirona.",
    "en": "Grannus was an ancient Celtic god of healing tied to springs and curative waters, identified with Apollo in Roman times and paired with the goddess Sirona.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "grannus-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Grannus (juga Grannos) adalah dewa penyembuhan Kelt (Celtic) kuno yang dikaitkan dengan mata air dan air penyembuh. Ia dipuja di Galia (Gaul), dua provinsi Germania (Germanies), Raetia, dan provinsi Danube, dengan persembahan tersebar dari Britania (Britain), Hispania, Roma (Rome), dan seberang perbatasan Rhine. Pada masa Romawi (Roman) ia paling sering disamakan dengan Apollo melalui interpretatio romana dan muncul dalam prasasti sebagai Apollo Grannus; pendampingnya yang biasa adalah dewi Sirona. Namanya tercatat dalam sekitar tiga puluh prasasti Latin, sebuah prasasti Yunani dari Ephesus, dan oleh sejarawan Cassius Dio.",
      "en": "Grannus (also Grannos) was an old Celtic god of healing linked with springs and healing waters. He was honoured in Gaul, both Germanies, Raetia and the Danube lands, with scattered dedications from Britain, Hispania, Rome and beyond the Rhine. In the Roman era he was most often matched with Apollo through the interpretatio romana and is named in inscriptions as Apollo Grannus; his usual partner was the goddess Sirona. He appears in about thirty Latin dedications, a Greek inscription from Ephesus and the history of Cassius Dio.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "grannus-c01",
        "grannus-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal namanya diperdebatkan; satu tafsiran mengaitkannya dengan kata Proto-Kelt untuk \"janggut\", sehingga berarti kira-kira \"yang berjanggut\". Aquae Granni (\"perairan Grannus\") adalah asal nama kota Aachen, dan mata air panas di sana diduga dipersembahkan kepadanya. Cassius Dio mencatat bahwa kaisar Caracalla memohon kesembuhan kepada Apollo Grannus, bersama Asclepius dan Serapis, tanpa hasil. Menurut artikel de, Grannus tampaknya dewa Kelt dengan penyebaran terluas; pusat pemujaannya di Germania Bawah, Germania Atas, dan Raetia, dan di Faimingen berdiri kuil Apollo-Grannus yang didirikan Caracalla.",
      "en": "The source of his name is debated; one reading ties it to a Proto-Celtic word for 'beard', making him roughly 'the bearded one'. Aquae Granni ('the waters of Grannus') underlies the name of Aachen, whose hot springs are thought to have been his. Cassius Dio records that the emperor Caracalla sought healing from Apollo Grannus, together with Asclepius and Serapis, to no avail. According to the de article Grannus seems to have been the most widespread Celtic god; his cult centred on Lower and Upper Germany and Raetia, and at Faimingen stood an Apollo-Grannus temple endowed by Caracalla.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "grannus-c03",
        "grannus-c04",
        "grannus-c05",
        "grannus-c07",
        "grannus-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": {
    "who": {
      "id": "Dewa penyembuhan Kelt.",
      "en": "Celtic healing god.",
