  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "grannus-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grannus",
      "title": "Grannus",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-s2",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grannus",
      "title": "Grannus",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "de",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "grannus-c01",
      "source_id": "grannus-s1",
      "quote": "was an ancient Celtic healing god associated with springs and curative waters. He was worshipped in Gaul, the two Germanies, Raetia and the Danube provinces, with scattered dedications from Britain, Hispania, Rome and beyond the Rhine frontier. In the Roman period he was most often identified with Apollo through the interpretatio romana and appears in inscriptions as Apollo Grannus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grannus (Grannos) dewa penyembuhan Kelt (Celtic), dipuja di Galia (Gaul), Germania (Germanies), Raetia, Danube, juga Britania (Britain), Hispania, Roma (Rome), Rhine; Romawi (Roman) menyamakannya dengan Apollo (Apollo Grannus); di Prancis dan Jerman.",
        "en": "Grannus (Grannos) was a Celtic healing god of Gaul, the Germanies, Raetia and the Danube, with dedications from Britain, Hispania, Rome; identified with Apollo (Apollo Grannus)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c02",
      "source_id": "grannus-s1",
      "quote": "He is named in about thirty Latin dedications, in a Greek honorific inscription from Ephesus, and by the historian Cassius Dio, who records that the emperor Caracalla sought his aid.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Disebut dalam sekitar tiga puluh prasasti Latin (Latin), prasasti Yunani (Greek) di Ephesus, dan oleh Cassius Dio.",
        "en": "Named in about thirty Latin dedications, a Greek inscription from Ephesus, and by Cassius Dio."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c03",
      "source_id": "grannus-s1",
      "quote": "One line of interpretation connects the name with Proto-Celtic *grand- (or *grend- ), meaning 'beard'. On this reading Grannus would mean roughly 'the bearded one'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dikaitkan dengan Proto-Kelt (Proto-Celtic) untuk janggut, yang berjanggut.",
        "en": "The name is linked to Proto-Celtic for beard, the bearded one."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c04",
      "source_id": "grannus-s1",
      "quote": "Place-names include Aquae Granni ('the waters of Grannus'), which lies behind the modern name of Aachen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aquae Granni, perairan Grannus, asal nama Aachen.",
        "en": "Aquae Granni, the waters of Grannus, lies behind the name of Aachen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c05",
      "source_id": "grannus-s1",
      "quote": "Hot springs such as those at Aquae Granni (today's Aachen) are thought to have been dedicated to Grannus. The clearest statement of Grannus's character comes from Cassius Dio, who lists Apollo Grannus with Asclepius and Serapis among the healing gods to whom Caracalla appealed, without relief, during his illness.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mata air panas Aquae Granni (Aachen) diduga untuk Grannus; Cassius Dio: Caracalla memohon kepada Apollo Grannus, Asclepius, Serapis tanpa hasil; di Jerman. (German)",
        "en": "Hot springs at Aquae Granni (Aachen) were likely his; Cassius Dio: Caracalla appealed to Apollo Grannus, Asclepius and Serapis without relief. (German)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c06",
      "source_id": "grannus-s2",
      "quote": "ist der Name eines Heilgottes der Kelten und wurde nach der Interpretatio Romana mit Apollon gleichgesetzt. Seine Partnerin war die Göttin Sirona.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grannus (Grannos) dewa penyembuh Kelt (Heilgottes der Kelten), disamakan dengan Apollon menurut Interpretatio Romana; pasangannya Sirona.",
        "en": "Grannus (Grannos) was a Celtic healing god (Heilgottes der Kelten), equated with Apollon by the Interpretatio Romana; his partner was Sirona."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c07",
      "source_id": "grannus-s2",
      "quote": "Grannus war offenbar der keltische Gott mit der größten Ausbreitung. Das Zentrum seiner Verehrung lag in den Provinzen Niedergermanien, Obergermanien und Raetia, inklusive der Agri decumates.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grannus dewa Kelt (keltische Gott) dengan penyebaran terluas; pusat di Germania Bawah (Niedergermanien), Germania Atas (Obergermanien), Raetia.",
        "en": "Grannus was the most widespread Celtic god; centred in Lower (Niedergermanien) and Upper Germany (Obergermanien) and Raetia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grannus-c08",
      "source_id": "grannus-s2",
      "quote": "In Faimingen steht der von Kaiser Caracalla gestiftete Apollo-Grannus-Tempel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Faimingen berdiri kuil Apollo-Grannus (Apollo-Grannus-Tempel) yang didirikan kaisar (Kaiser) Caracalla.",
        "en": "At Faimingen stands the Apollo-Grannus temple (Apollo-Grannus-Tempel) endowed by Emperor (Kaiser) Caracalla."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Liver bird adalah makhluk mitos yang menjadi lambang kota Liverpool di Inggris, biasanya digambarkan sebagai burung kormoran.",
    "en": "The liver bird is a mythical creature that serves as the symbol of the English city of Liverpool, usually shown as a cormorant.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "liver-bird-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Liver bird adalah makhluk mitos yang menjadi lambang kota Liverpool di Inggris (English). Biasanya ia digambarkan sebagai kormoran, dan dalam lambang kota ia membawa seikat rumput laut laver di paruhnya sebagai permainan kata lain atas nama Liverpool. Segel kuno Liverpool, yang cetakan tertuanya dari 1352, menunjukkan burung umum dengan ranting di paruh dan gulungan bertuliskan \"JOHIS\"; burung itu hampir pasti dimaksudkan sebagai elang, lambang Yohanes Penginjil. Pada abad ke-17 identitas aslinya terlupakan, dan burung itu mulai ditafsirkan sebagai kormoran atau \"lever\".",
      "en": "The liver bird is a mythical being that stands as the emblem of the English city of Liverpool. It is usually drawn as a cormorant and, on the city arms, holds a sprig of laver seaweed in its beak, another play on the name Liverpool. Liverpool's old seal, whose earliest surviving impression dates from 1352, showed a generic bird with a sprig in its beak and a scroll reading 'JOHIS'; the bird was almost surely meant as an eagle, emblem of John the Evangelist. By the 17th century its real identity had been forgotten and it came to be read as a cormorant or a 'lever'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "liver-bird-c01",
        "liver-bird-c02",
        "liver-bird-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Popularitas modern lambang ini berasal dari 1911, ketika Royal Liver Building dibangun dengan dua liver bird di puncaknya, yang menghidupkan kembali gagasan bahwa liver adalah burung mitos yang dahulu menghantui pesisir setempat. Menurut legenda populer, keduanya sepasang jantan dan betina: betina memandang ke laut menunggu pelaut pulang dengan selamat, jantan memandang ke kota menjaga keluarga mereka. Legenda setempat juga menyebut kedua burung saling membelakangi, sebab jika mereka kawin dan terbang pergi, kota itu akan lenyap; cerita lain menyebut mereka dirantai karena jika terbang, Sungai Mersey akan meluap dan membanjiri Liverpool.",
      "en": "The symbol's modern fame dates to 1911, when the Royal Liver Building went up crowned with two liver birds, which brought back the notion of the liver as a legendary bird once haunting the nearby coast. Popular legend makes them a male and female: she gazes out to sea awaiting the sailors' safe return, he looks over the city guarding their families. Local legend also says the birds face away from each other, for if they mated and flew away the city would cease to exist; another story says they are chained down, since if they flew off the Mersey would burst its banks and flood Liverpool.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "liver-bird-c04",
        "liver-bird-c05",
        "liver-bird-c06",
        "liver-bird-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "is a mythical creature that is the symbol of the English city of Liverpool. It is normally represented as a cormorant, and appears as such on the city's arms, in which it bears a branch of laver seaweed in its beak as a further pun on the name \"Liverpool\".",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "liver-bird-c02",
      "source_id": "liver-bird-s1",
      "quote": "The seal depicted a generic bird with a plant sprig in its beak, together with a scroll inscribed (in shaky letters) \"JOHIS\" - an abbreviation for Johannis, Latin for \"John's\". The bird was almost certainly intended to be an eagle, the symbol of John the Evangelist",

      "source_id": "liver-bird-s1",
      "quote": "By the 17th century the bird's real identity had been forgotten: it began to be interpreted either as a cormorant, a common bird in the area, or as a \"lever\".",

    {
      "id": "liver-bird-c04",
      "source_id": "liver-bird-s1",
      "quote": "The modern popularity of the symbol largely dates to 1911, when the Liver Building was built. This prominent display of two liver birds rekindled the idea that the liver was a mythical bird that once haunted the local shoreline.",

      "context": "historical-record",

    {
      "id": "liver-bird-c05",
      "source_id": "liver-bird-s1",
      "quote": "According to popular legend, they are a male and female pair: the female looking out to sea, watching for the seamen to return safely home, and the male looking in to the city, watching over the seamen's families",

      "id": "liver-bird-c06",
      "source_id": "liver-bird-s1",
      "quote": "Local legend also holds that the birds face away from each other, for if they were to mate and fly away, the city would cease to exist.",

      "source_id": "liver-bird-s2",

      "quote": "Der Liver Bird ( ˈlaɪvər bɜrd, dt. etwa: Livervogel) ist ein mythologisches Wesen und Symbolfigur der englischen Stadt Liverpool.",

    "id": "Llŷr kemungkinan adalah dewa laut dalam mitologi Wales, berkerabat dengan Ler dari Irlandia, dan ayah Brân, Brânwen, serta Manawydan.",
    "en": "Llŷr was probably a sea god of Welsh myth, related to the Irish Ler, and father of Brân, Brânwen and Manawydan.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "llyr-c01",
      "llyr-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Llŷr (Wales/Welsh: Llŷr Llediaith; Lleddiaith berarti \"setengah bicara\" atau \"setengah bahasa\") kemungkinan adalah dewa laut dalam mitologi Wales, berkerabat dengan Ler dari Irlandia (Irish), \"Sang Laut\", ayah Manannán mac Lir. Dalam sebagian legenda ia pemimpin Children of Llyr yang terus berperang dengan Children of Dôn. Dalam Branwen, Daughter of Llyr, Cabang Kedua Mabinogi, ia ayah Brân, Brânwen, dan Manawydan dari Penarddun. Welsh Triads menyebut Llŷr dipenjarakan oleh Euroswydd, yang kemudian tampaknya menikahi Penarddun dan menjadi ayah Nisien dan Efnisien.",
      "en": "Llŷr (Welsh Llŷr Llediaith; Lleddiaith meaning 'half-speech' or 'half-language') was probably a sea god of Welsh myth, kin to the Irish Ler, 'the Sea', father of Manannán mac Lir. Some legends make him leader of the Children of Llyr, forever at war with the Children of Dôn. In Branwen, Daughter of Llyr, the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, he is the father of Brân, Brânwen and Manawydan by Penarddun. The Welsh Triads say Llŷr was jailed by Euroswydd, who apparently then wed Penarddun and fathered Nisien and Efnisien.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "llyr-c01",
        "llyr-c02",
        "llyr-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lakon William Shakespeare King Lear berdasar secara tidak langsung pada raja mitos Geoffrey of Monmouth, King Leir, yang sering dikaitkan dengan Llŷr meski kemungkinan tidak berhubungan. Menurut artikel de, llyr dalam bahasa Wales berarti \"laut\" dan Llŷr adalah padanan Wales dari dewa laut Irlandia Lir; baik Llŷr maupun Lir tampaknya punya kedekatan dengan burung, karena nama anak-anak Llŷr memuat lambang gagak. Ia juga disebut salah satu dari \"tiga tahanan agung Pulau Britania\".",
      "en": "William Shakespeare's King Lear rests, at second hand, on Geoffrey of Monmouth's mythical King Leir, often linked with Llŷr though probably unrelated. According to the de article, llyr means 'sea' in Welsh and Llŷr is the Welsh counterpart of the Irish sea god Lir; both seem to have a bird affinity, since the names of Llŷr's children carry raven symbolism. He is also named one of the 'three exalted prisoners of the Isle of Britain'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "llyr-c04",
        "llyr-c05",
        "llyr-c06",
        "llyr-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The Welsh Triads states that Llŷr was imprisoned by Euroswydd, and presumably, Penarddun subsequently married Euroswydd, giving birth by Euroswydd to her two younger sons, Nisien and Efnisien, as stated in the Second Branch.",

      "source_id": "llyr-s1",
      "quote": "William Shakespeare's play King Lear is based on material taken secondhand (through Raphael Holinshed) from Geoffrey of Monmouth's mythical king King Leir, who has often been connected, but is likely unrelated, to Llŷr.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Redcap adalah goblin jahat dan pembunuh dalam cerita rakyat wilayah perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia, yang merendam topinya dalam darah korbannya.",
    "en": "The redcap is a malevolent, murderous goblin of the Anglo-Scottish border folklore who soaks his cap in his victims' blood.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "redcap-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Redcap (atau powrie) adalah sejenis goblin jahat dan pembunuh dalam cerita rakyat wilayah perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia (Anglo-Scottish). Ia dikatakan menghuni kastel-kastel runtuh di sepanjang perbatasan, terutama yang pernah menjadi tempat tirani atau kejahatan, dan terkenal merendam topinya dalam darah korban. Ia juga dikenal sebagai Redcomb dan Bloody Cap. Ia digambarkan sebagai elf tua pendek kekar dengan gigi panjang menonjol, jari kurus bercakar seperti elang, mata besar merah menyala, rambut kusut tergerai, sepatu bot besi, tongkat tombak di tangan kiri, dan topi merah di kepalanya.",
      "en": "The redcap (or powrie) is a kind of wicked, murderous goblin in the lore of the Anglo-Scottish border. It is said to haunt ruined castles along the border, above all those that saw tyranny or foul deeds, and is known for steeping its cap in its victims' blood. Other names for it are Redcomb and Bloody Cap. It is pictured as a short, stocky old elf with long jutting teeth, thin fingers tipped with eagle-like talons, big fiery-red eyes, grisly hair down its shoulders, iron boots, a pikestaff in its left hand and a red cap on its head.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "redcap-c01",
        "redcap-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika pelancong berlindung di sarangnya, ia melempari mereka dengan batu besar, dan jika berhasil membunuh, ia merendam topinya dalam darah mereka hingga merah tua. Kekuatan manusia tak mempan terhadapnya, tetapi ia dapat diusir dengan ayat Kitab Suci atau salib, yang membuatnya menjerit lalu lenyap dalam api, meninggalkan sebuah gigi besar. Dalam cerita rakyat, bangsawan Skotlandia William de Soulis dikatakan memiliki redcap pembantu bernama Robin Redcap. Menurut artikel de, redcap harus terus mencari korban karena mereka mati begitu darah di topi mereka mengering.",
      "en": "When travellers shelter in its lair it hurls great stones at them, and if it kills them it soaks its cap in their blood, dyeing it crimson. Human strength cannot harm it, but Scripture or a raised crucifix drives it off, making it give a dismal yell and vanish in flames, leaving a large tooth. Folklore gave the Scottish nobleman William de Soulis a redcap familiar called Robin Redcap. According to the de article redcaps must keep finding victims because they die as soon as the blood in their caps dries.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "redcap-c03",
        "redcap-c04",
        "redcap-c05",
        "redcap-c07"
      ]
    }

        "id": "Redcap (Powrie, Dunter) dalam folklore Britania (britischen) goblin haus darah, kobold, elf, atau peri (Fee).",
        "en": "The Redcap (Powrie, Dunter) of British (britischen) folklore is a bloodthirsty goblin, kobold, elf or fairy (Fee)."

    },
    {
      "id": "redcap-c07",
      "source_id": "redcap-s2",
      "quote": "Redcaps müssen regelmäßig Opfer finden, da sie sterben, sobald das Blut in ihren Mützen austrocknet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Redcap harus terus mencari korban karena mati bila darah di topinya (Mützen) kering.",
        "en": "Redcaps must find victims regularly, as they die when the blood in their caps (Mützen) dries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "redcap-c08",
      "source_id": "redcap-s2",
      "quote": "Er richtete viel Schaden an und zerstörte den Wohnsitz seines Herrn, Hermitage Castle.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Robin Redcap merusak kediaman tuannya, Hermitage Castle.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Twrch Trwyth adalah babi hutan ajaib dalam legenda Arthurian, yang perburuannya oleh Raja Arthur dikisahkan dalam Culhwch and Olwen.",
    "en": "Twrch Trwyth is a fabulous wild boar of Arthurian legend whose hunt by King Arthur is told in Culhwch and Olwen.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "twrch-trwyth-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Twrch Trwyth (juga Trwyd dalam bahasa Wales/Welsh) adalah babi hutan ajaib dalam legenda Arthurian. Kisah perburuannya yang sangat terperinci terdapat dalam roman prosa Wales Culhwch and Olwen, kemungkinan ditulis sekitar 1100. Perburuan itu melibatkan Raja Arthur (King Arthur), pasukannya, dan anjingnya Cafall, serta memerlukan orang-orang berbakat, anjing lain, dan perlengkapan seperti tali kekang, sesuai tugas yang ditetapkan raksasa Ysbaddaden. Babi itu dibawa keluar dari Irlandia (Ireland), digiring ke Britania (Britain), lalu didorong dari tebing ke laut di Cornwall.",
      "en": "Twrch Trwyth (also Welsh Trwyd) is a marvellous wild boar of Arthurian legend. The Welsh prose romance Culhwch and Olwen, likely written around 1100, gives a lavish account of its hunt. King Arthur, his host and his hound Cafall took part, and the chase also called for gifted men, more hounds and gear such as a leash, by the tasks the giant Ysbaddaden set. The boar was driven out of Ireland, chased across Britain and at last forced over a cliff into the sea in Cornwall.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "twrch-trwyth-c01",
        "twrch-trwyth-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda dalam bentuk sederhananya jauh lebih tua: perburuan babi hutan Troynt oleh Arthur dengan anjingnya tampak dalam Historia Brittonum abad ke-9, dan sebuah rujukan dalam elegi Gwarchan Cynfelyn (abad ke-7) di Book of Aneirin dianggap sebutan tertuanya dalam sastra. Namanya dapat dibaca sebagai \"babi Trwyth\", dan para sarjana dahulu menganggapnya padanan babi Triath dalam mitologi Irlandia. Menurut artikel de, babi adalah hewan ternak terpenting bangsa Kelt, dan Twrch Trwyth bersama Ysgithyrwyn adalah babi hutan paling terkenal di Wales.",
      "en": "The legend in simpler form is much older: Arthur hunting the boar Troynt with his hound appears in the ninth-century Historia Brittonum, and a passing mention in the seventh-century elegy Gwarchan Cynfelyn in the Book of Aneirin is thought its earliest literary trace. The name can be read 'the boar Trwyth', and earlier scholars likened it to the boar Triath of Irish myth. According to the de article pigs were the Celts' most important livestock, and Twrch Trwyth and Ysgithyrwyn are the most famous boars of Wales.",

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Brown Lady of Raynham Hall adalah hantu yang konon menghantui Raynham Hall di Norfolk, Inggris, dan menjadi terkenal lewat foto majalah Country Life tahun 1936.",
    "en": "The Brown Lady of Raynham Hall is a ghost said to haunt Raynham Hall in Norfolk, England, made famous by a 1936 Country Life photograph.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c01",
      "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Brown Lady of Raynham Hall adalah hantu yang konon menghantui Raynham Hall di Norfolk, Inggris (England). Ia menjadi salah satu kisah hantu paling terkenal di Britania Raya (United Kingdom) setelah fotografer majalah Country Life mengaku memotretnya. Julukan \"Brown Lady\" berasal dari gaun brokat cokelat yang konon dikenakannya. Menurut legenda, penampakan itu adalah Dorothy Walpole (1686–1726), saudari Robert Walpole, yang umumnya dianggap Perdana Menteri pertama Britania Raya; ia istri kedua Charles Townshend, yang konon mengurungnya di kamar di Raynham Hall setelah mengetahui perselingkuhannya dengan Lord Wharton.",
      "en": "The Brown Lady of Raynham Hall is a ghost reputed to haunt Raynham Hall in Norfolk, England. It grew into one of the best-known hauntings in the United Kingdom after Country Life photographers said they had caught it on film. She takes her name from the brown brocade gown she is said to wear. Legend holds she is Dorothy Walpole (1686–1726), sister of Robert Walpole, who is usually counted Great Britain's first Prime Minister; Charles Townshend, whose second wife she was, reportedly locked her in her rooms at Raynham Hall on learning of her affair with Lord Wharton.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c01",
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c02",
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penampakan pertama yang tercatat dilaporkan Lucia C. Stone tentang perayaan Natal 1835 di Raynham Hall: Kolonel Loftus dan seorang tamu bernama Hawkins mengaku melihatnya, dan malam berikutnya Loftus melihat rongga matanya yang kosong. Pada 1836 Kapten Frederick Marryat, sahabat Charles Dickens, juga dikatakan melihatnya. Menurut artikel de, tidak ada bukti keaslian foto 1936 maupun penampakan lainnya, dan para ilmuwan skeptis masa kini, termasuk Joe Nickell, berpendapat foto itu dipalsukan, mungkin dengan pencahayaan ganda.",
      "en": "The first recorded sighting was reported by Lucia C. Stone of a Christmas party at Raynham Hall in 1835: Colonel Loftus and a guest named Hawkins said they saw her, and the next night Loftus noticed her empty eye sockets. In 1836 Captain Frederick Marryat, a friend of Charles Dickens, is also said to have seen her. According to the de article there is no proof that the 1936 photo or other sightings are genuine, and modern sceptics including Joe Nickell think the picture was faked, perhaps by double exposure.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c04",
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c05",
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c06",
        "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c07"
      ]
    }

      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda hantu itu Dorothy Walpole (1686–1726), saudari Robert Walpole, Perdana Menteri pertama (first Prime Minister) Britania Raya (Great Britain).",
        "en": "Legend says the ghost is Dorothy Walpole (1686–1726), sister of Robert Walpole, first Prime Minister of Great Britain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c03",
      "source_id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-s1",
      "quote": "The story says that when Townshend discovered that his wife had committed adultery with Lord Wharton, he punished her by locking her in her rooms in the family home, Raynham Hall.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Charles Townshend mengurungnya di Raynham Hall karena berselingkuh dengan Lord Wharton.",
        "en": "Charles Townshend locked her in Raynham Hall for adultery with Lord Wharton."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c04",
      "source_id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-s1",
      "quote": "Loftus and another guest named Hawkins said they had seen the \"Brown Lady\" one night as they approached their bedrooms, noting in particular the dated brown dress she wore. The following evening Loftus claimed to have seen the \"Brown Lady\" again, later reporting that on this occasion he was drawn to the spectre's empty eye-sockets",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lucia C. Stone: Natal 1835, Kolonel Loftus dan Hawkins melihat Brown Lady; Loftus melihat rongga mata kosong.",
        "en": "Lucia C. Stone: at Christmas 1835 Colonel Loftus and Hawkins saw the Brown Lady; Loftus noted her empty eye sockets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c05",
      "source_id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-s1",
      "quote": "The next reported sighting of the \"Brown Lady\" was made in 1836 by Captain Frederick Marryat, a friend of novelist Charles Dickens, and the author of a series of popular sea novels.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan 1836 oleh Kapten (Captain) Frederick Marryat, sahabat Charles Dickens.",
        "en": "An 1836 sighting by Captain Frederick Marryat, friend of Charles Dickens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c06",
      "source_id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-s2",
      "quote": "Beweise für die Echtheit des Fotos oder weiterer Sichtungen gibt es nicht. Heutige skeptische Wissenschaftler, darunter Joe Nickell, gehen davon aus, dass das Bild gefälscht ist, möglicherweise durch Mehrfachbelichtung.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak ada bukti keaslian foto (1936, Country Life); ilmuwan skeptis seperti Joe Nickell menganggapnya palsu, mungkin pencahayaan ganda (Mehrfachbelichtung).",
        "en": "No proof the photo (1936, Country Life) is genuine; sceptics such as Joe Nickell think it faked, perhaps by multiple exposure (Mehrfachbelichtung)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-c07",
      "source_id": "brown-lady-of-raynham-hall-s1",
      "quote": "The first recorded claim of a sighting of the ghost was by Lucia C. Stone concerning a gathering at Raynham Hall in the Christmas of 1835.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan pertama tercatat oleh Lucia C. Stone pada Natal 1835 di Raynham Hall.",
        "en": "First recorded sighting by Lucia C. Stone at Christmas 1835 at Raynham Hall."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Crom Cruach adalah dewa pagan Irlandia pra-Kristen yang menurut penulis Kristen dipuaskan dengan kurban manusia, sampai pemujaannya diakhiri Santo Patrick.",
    "en": "Crom Cruach was a pagan god of pre-Christian Ireland who, Christian writers say, was appeased with human sacrifice until Saint Patrick ended his worship.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "crom-cruach-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Crom Cruach (Irlandia Kuno/Old Irish: Cromm Crúaich) adalah dewa pagan Irlandia (Ireland) pra-Kristen. Menurut penulis Kristen (Christian), ia dipuaskan dengan kurban manusia dan pemujaannya diakhiri oleh Santo Patrick (Saint Patrick). Ia juga disebut Crom Cróich, Cenn Cruach, dan Cenncroithi, dan berhubungan dengan tokoh kemudian Crom Dubh. Rujukan dalam puisi dinnsenchas abad ke-12 tentang kurban sebagai ganti susu dan gandum menunjukkan perannya sebagai dewa kesuburan; gambarannya sebagai sosok emas dikelilingi dua belas sosok batu atau perunggu ditafsirkan sebagian orang sebagai matahari dikelilingi zodiak.",
      "en": "Crom Cruach (Old Irish Cromm Crúaich) was a pagan god of Ireland in pre-Christian times. Christian authors say he was placated with human sacrifice and that Saint Patrick put an end to his cult. He is also called Crom Cróich, Cenn Cruach and Cenncroithi, and is related to the later figure Crom Dubh. A 12th-century dinnsenchas poem speaks of sacrifice in return for milk and grain, suggesting a fertility role; his image of a gold figure ringed by twelve stone or bronze ones has been read by some as the sun among the signs of the zodiac.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "crom-cruach-c01",
        "crom-cruach-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut puisi dinsenchas dalam Book of Leinster, arca pemujaannya berdiri di Magh Slécht (\"dataran sujud\") di County Cavan dan dipuaskan dengan kurban anak sulung demi hasil susu dan gandum yang baik. Raja Agung awal Tigernmas, bersama tiga perempat pasukannya, dikatakan mati saat memujanya pada malam Samhain, tetapi pemujaan berlanjut sampai arca itu dihancurkan Santo Patrick dengan palu godam. Menurut artikel de, ia digambarkan sebagai dewa dunia bawah dan kematian, dan kadang dicampuradukkan dengan dewa kesuburan Cromm Dubh.",
      "en": "By a dinsenchas poem in the Book of Leinster, his cult image stood on Magh Slécht ('the plain of prostration') in County Cavan and was offered first-born children for good yields of milk and grain. The early High King Tigernmas, with three quarters of his host, is said to have died worshipping him on Samhain eve, yet the cult went on until Saint Patrick smashed the image with a sledgehammer. According to the de article he is cast as a god of the underworld and death, and is sometimes merged with the fertility god Cromm Dubh.",

        "crom-cruach-c03",

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dökkálfar (\"Elf Gelap\") dan Ljósálfar (\"Elf Terang\") adalah dua jenis elf yang berlawanan dalam mitologi Nordik, yang satu tinggal di dalam bumi dan yang lain di Álfheimr.",
    "en": "The Dökkálfar ('Dark Elves') and Ljósálfar ('Light Elves') are two contrasting kinds of elves in Norse myth, one dwelling in the earth and the other in Álfheimr.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (Norse mythology), Dökkálfar (\"Dark Elves\") dan Ljósálfar (\"Light Elves\") adalah dua jenis elf yang berlawanan: elf gelap tinggal di dalam bumi dan berkulit gelap, sedangkan elf terang tinggal di Álfheimr dan \"lebih elok dari matahari untuk dipandang\". Keduanya tercatat dalam Prose Edda karya Snorri Sturluson abad ke-13 dan dalam puisi Norse Kuno akhir Hrafnagaldr Óðins. Dalam bab 17 Gylfaginning, High menyebut Ljósálfar tinggal di Álfheimr, sedangkan Dökkálfar tinggal di bawah tanah dan tampak, terlebih berperilaku, sangat berbeda; elf terang \"lebih elok dari matahari\", elf gelap \"lebih hitam dari ter\".",
      "en": "In Norse myth the Dökkálfar ('Dark Elves') and Ljósálfar ('Light Elves') are two opposed kinds of elves: the dark ones live inside the earth and are dark-skinned, while the light ones dwell in Álfheimr and outshine the sun in beauty. Both appear in the Prose Edda, written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century, and in the late Old Norse poem Hrafnagaldr Óðins. In chapter 17 of Gylfaginning, High says the Ljósálfar dwell in Álfheimr while the Dökkálfar live underground and differ greatly in looks and still more in conduct; the light are 'fairer than the sun', the dark 'blacker than pitch'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c01",
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c02",
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "High juga menyebut bahwa langit-langit lain di atas, Andlàngr dan Víðbláinn tempat Gimlé berada, untuk sementara hanya dihuni elf terang. Karena konsep ini hanya tercatat dalam Gylfaginning dan Hrafnagaldr Óðins, tidak jelas apakah pembedaan dua jenis elf berasal dari Snorri sendiri; sebagian sarjana, seperti Anne Holtsmark dengan ragu, menduganya hasil pengaruh Kristen tentang malaikat terang dan gelap. Terjemahan Anderson atas Younger Edda (Gutenberg) berbunyi: elf terang tinggal di Alfheim, elf gelap di dalam bumi, berbeda rupa dan lebih berbeda lagi dalam perbuatan.",
      "en": "High also says the further heavens above, Andlàngr and Víðbláinn where Gimlé stands, are for now inhabited only by light elves. Since the idea is found only in Gylfaginning and Hrafnagaldr Óðins, it is unclear whether Snorri devised the split himself; some scholars, Anne Holtsmark among them with reservations, think it reflects Christian ideas of angels of light and darkness. Anderson's translation of the Younger Edda (Gutenberg) reads that the light-elves live in Alfheim and the dark-elves down in the earth, unlike in looks and more so in deeds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c04",
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c06",
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c07",
        "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Menurut Prose Edda, elf terang \"lebih elok dari matahari\" sedangkan elf gelap \"lebih hitam dari ter\".",
    "en": "In the Prose Edda light elves are 'fairer than the sun' while dark elves are 'blacker than pitch'.",

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%B6kk%C3%A1lfar_and_Lj%C3%B3s%C3%A1lfar",
      "title": "Dökkálfar and Ljósálfar",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s2",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/files/18947/18947-h/18947-h.htm",
      "title": "https://www.gutenberg.org/files/18947/18947-h/18947-h.htm",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "www.gutenberg.org",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "other",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c01",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Dökkálfar (\"Dark Elves\") and Ljósálfar (\"Light Elves\") are two contrasting types of elves; the dark elves dwell within the earth and have a dark complexion, while the light elves live in Álfheimr, and are \"fairer than the sun to look at\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (Norse mythology), Dökkálfar (Dark Elves) dan Ljósálfar (Light Elves) dua jenis elf; elf gelap di dalam bumi, elf terang di Álfheimr.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology the Dökkálfar (Dark Elves) and Ljósálfar (Light Elves) are two kinds of elves; dark in the earth, light in Álfheimr."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c02",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "The Ljósálfar and the Dökkálfar are attested in the Prose Edda, written in the 13th century by Snorri Sturluson, and in the late Old Norse poem Hrafnagaldr Óðins.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tercatat dalam Prose Edda karya Snorri Sturluson (abad ke-13) dan puisi Norse Kuno (Old Norse) Hrafnagaldr Óðins; di Islandia.",
        "en": "Attested in Snorri Sturluson's 13th-century Prose Edda and the Old Norse poem Hrafnagaldr Óðins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c03",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "High says that the Ljósálfar live in Álfheimr, while the Dökkálfar dwell underground and look—and particularly behave—quite unlike the Ljósálfar. High describes the Ljósálfar as \"fairer than the sun to look at\", while the Dökkálfar are \"blacker than pitch\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bab 17 Gylfaginning, High: Ljósálfar di Álfheimr, Dökkálfar di bawah tanah; terang lebih elok dari matahari, gelap lebih hitam dari ter.",
        "en": "In Gylfaginning ch. 17 High says the Ljósálfar live in Álfheimr, the Dökkálfar underground; light fairer than the sun, dark blacker than pitch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c04",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "High adds that \"we believe it is only light-elves who inhabit these places for the time being\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "High: langit lain (Andlàngr, Víðbláinn, Gimlé) untuk sementara hanya dihuni elf terang.",
        "en": "High: the other heavens (Andlàngr, Víðbláinn, Gimlé) are for now inhabited only by light elves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c05",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s2",
      "quote": "There dwell the folk that are called light-elves; but the dark-elves dwell down in the earth, and they are unlike the light-elves in appearance, but much more so in deeds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Anderson (Younger Edda, Gutenberg): elf terang di Alfheim, elf gelap di dalam bumi, berbeda rupa dan perbuatan.",
        "en": "Anderson's translation (Younger Edda, Gutenberg): light-elves in Alfheim, dark-elves in the earth, unlike in appearance and deeds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c06",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "As the concept is only recorded in Gylfaginning and the late poem Hrafnagaldr Óðins, it is unclear whether the distinction between the two types of elves originated with Snorri, or if he was merely recounting a concept already developed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak jelas apakah pembedaan ini berasal dari Snorri.",
        "en": "It is unclear whether the distinction originated with Snorri."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-c07",
      "source_id": "dokkalfar-and-ljosalfar-s1",
      "quote": "The sub-classification perhaps resulted from Christian influence, by way of importation of the concept of good and evil and angels of light and darkness. Anne Holtsmark aired this view, though with some reservation",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mungkin pengaruh Kristen (Christian), malaikat terang dan gelap; pendapat Anne Holtsmark.",
        "en": "Perhaps Christian influence, angels of light and darkness; Anne Holtsmark's view."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Tidak ada gambar dengan lisensi yang diperiksa."
    },

      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Leanan sídhe (\"kekasih peri\") adalah sosok mitologi Kelt, perempuan cantik dari Aos Sí yang mengambil kekasih manusia; para kekasihnya hidup singkat tetapi penuh ilham.",
    "en": "The leanan sídhe ('fairy lover') is a figure of Celtic myth, a beautiful woman of the Aos Sí who takes a human lover; her lovers live brief but inspired lives.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "leanan-sidhe-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Leannán sídhe (Irlandia/Irish, harfiah \"kekasih peri\"; Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic: leannan sìth; Manx: lhiannan shee) adalah sosok dari mitologi Kelt (Celtic). Ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan cantik dari Aos Sí (\"orang gundukan peri\") yang mengambil kekasih manusia, dan para kekasihnya dikatakan hidup singkat tetapi sangat terilhami. Namanya berasal dari kata Gaelik untuk kekasih dan sebutan bagi penghuni gundukan peri. Meski biasanya berwujud peri perempuan, ada setidaknya satu rujukan tentang leannán sídhe laki-laki yang mengganggu perempuan fana.",
      "en": "The leannán sídhe (Irish, literally 'fairy lover'; Scottish Gaelic leannan sìth; Manx lhiannan shee) comes from Celtic myth. She is shown as a lovely woman of the Aos Sí ('people of the fairy mounds') who chooses a human lover, and those lovers are said to live short but deeply inspired lives. The name joins Gaelic words for sweetheart and for the dwellers of fairy mounds. Though usually a female fairy, there is at least one account of a male leannán sídhe troubling a mortal woman.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "leanan-sidhe-c01",
        "leanan-sidhe-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah versi mitos ini dipopulerkan selama Kebangkitan Kelt (Celtic Revival) akhir abad kesembilan belas; Jane Wilde (\"Speranza\") menyebutnya dalam Ancient Legends, Mystic Charms and Superstitions of Ireland (1887). W. B. Yeats mempopulerkan versinya sendiri yang menonjolkan sifat hampir vampiriknya: ia muse cantik yang memberi ilham kepada seniman sebagai ganti cinta dan pengabdian, tetapi hubungan gaib itu berujung kegilaan dan kematian. Menurut artikel de, sosok serupa Lhiannan Shee dikenal di Isle of Man, dan para peri ini menghabiskan nyawa kekasihnya serta tak membiarkan mereka lolos bahkan setelah mati.",
      "en": "A version of the myth spread during the Celtic Revival of the late nineteenth century; Jane Wilde ('Speranza') mentions it in her Ancient Legends, Mystic Charms and Superstitions of Ireland (1887). W. B. Yeats popularised his own take stressing her near-vampiric side: a beautiful muse giving an artist inspiration in return for love and devotion, an affair that ends in madness and death. According to the de article a similar Lhiannan Shee is known on the Isle of Man, and these fairies use up their lovers' lives and let them go not even in death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "leanan-sidhe-c03",
        "leanan-sidhe-c04",
        "leanan-sidhe-c05",
        "leanan-sidhe-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Leannán sídhe (Irlandia/Irish, kekasih peri; Scottish Gaelic leannan sìth; Manx lhiannan shee) sosok mitologi Kelt (Celtic), perempuan Aos Sí yang mengambil kekasih manusia; di Irlandia. (Skotlandia)",

      "source_id": "leanan-sidhe-s1",

      "source_id": "leanan-sidhe-s1",
      "quote": "A version of the myth was popularized during the Celtic Revival in the late 19th-century. The leannán sídhe is mentioned by Jane Wilde, writing as \"Speranza\", in her 1887 Ancient Legends, Mystic Charms and Superstitions of Ireland.",

As he imagined it, the leannán sídhe is depicted as a beautiful muse who offers inspiration to an artist in exchange for their love and devotion; although the supernatural affair leads to madness and eventual death for the artist",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nabia (atau Navia) adalah dewi bangsa-bangsa pra-Romawi di Semenanjung Iberia, kadang ditafsirkan sebagai dewi air, sungai, lembah, hutan, atau kesuburan.",
    "en": "Known as Nabia or Navia, she was worshipped by peoples of Iberia in pre-Roman times and is read as a deity of water, rivers, valleys, forests or fertility.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nabia-c01",
      "nabia-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nabia (atau Navia) adalah dewi bangsa-bangsa pra-Romawi (Pre-Roman) di Semenanjung Iberia (Iberian Peninsula), dan pemujaannya juga meluas selama pendudukan Romawi (Roman). Ia dipuja di banyak tempat dengan cara yang sangat berbeda-beda, sehingga sebagian sejarawan menduga \"Nabia\" hanyalah kata umum yang dipakai berbagai bangsa untuk dewa-dewa mereka, sebuah teori yang dipertanyakan pihak lain. Karena sifatnya tidak pasti, ia kadang ditafsirkan sebagai dewi air, kadang dikaitkan dengan lembah, hutan, dan bukit, dan juga dipandang sebagai dewi kesuburan, kesehatan, dan kelimpahan. Namanya tercatat dalam 28 prasasti hingga 2025.",
      "en": "In pre-Roman times the peoples of the Iberian Peninsula worshipped Nabia (or Navia), and her cult also spread widely under Roman rule. She was honoured in many places in quite different ways, leading some historians to suspect 'Nabia' was merely a common word different peoples used for their gods, an idea others dispute. As her nature is unclear she is sometimes read as a water goddess, sometimes tied to valleys, woods and hills, and also seen as a goddess of fertility, health and plenty. Her name appears in 28 inscriptions as of 2025.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nabia-c01",
        "nabia-c02",
        "nabia-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel fr, Nabia atau Navia adalah dewi sungai dan air dalam mitologi Luso-Galicia, di wilayah Galicia dan Asturias (Spanyol) serta Portugal, salah satu dewa yang paling banyak tercatat dengan dua puluh prasasti di wilayah bangsa Galicia dan Lusitania. Dewi pra-Kelt asal Indo-Eropa ini umumnya dianggap dewi kesuburan. Sungai Navia dan Avia di Galicia dan Asturias serta sungai Neiva dekat Braga dikaitkan dengan namanya.",
      "en": "According to the fr article, Nabia or Navia was the goddess of rivers and water in Luso-Galician myth, across today's Galicia and Asturias (Spain) and Portugal, one of the best-attested deities with twenty inscriptions in lands of the Gallaeci and Lusitanians. This pre-Celtic goddess of Indo-European origin is generally seen as a fertility goddess. The rivers Navia and Avia in Galicia and Asturias and the Neiva near Braga are linked with her name.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nabia-c04",
        "nabia-c05",
        "nabia-c06",
        "nabia-c09"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Spriggan adalah makhluk legenda cerita rakyat Cornwall, terutama di West Penwith, yang menjaga harta terpendam di reruntuhan dan gundukan kubur.",
    "en": "The spriggan is a legendary creature of Cornish folklore, especially of West Penwith, that guards buried treasure at ruins and barrows.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "spriggan-c01",
      "spriggan-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Spriggan adalah makhluk legenda dari cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornish folklore), terutama dikaitkan dengan West Penwith di Cornwall. Kata ini diucapkan dengan bunyi j dan dipinjam dari bentuk jamak bahasa Cornwall spyrysyon, \"roh-roh\". Spriggan sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua keriput yang sangat buruk rupa dengan kepala besar seperti kepala anak, dan ditemukan di reruntuhan tua, tumpukan batu, dan gundukan kubur sambil menjaga harta terpendam. Meski bertubuh kecil, mereka sering dianggap hantu para raksasa yang masih memiliki kekuatan raksasa, dan dalam satu kisah yang dikumpulkan Robert Hunt mereka dapat membengkak menjadi sangat besar.",
      "en": "The spriggan is a legendary being of Cornish folklore, linked above all with West Penwith in Cornwall. The word is said with a soft g and is borrowed from the Cornish plural spyrysyon, 'spirits'. Spriggans are often shown as hideously ugly, shrivelled old men with big, childlike heads, haunting old ruins, cairns and barrows where they guard buried treasure. Though small, they were often thought to be ghosts of giants who kept a giant's strength, and in one tale gathered by Robert Hunt they could swell to huge size.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "spriggan-c01",
        "spriggan-c02",
        "spriggan-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Spriggan terkenal berwatak buruk dan suka mencelakai yang menyinggung mereka: mereka menimbulkan angin puyuh untuk menakuti pelancong, mengirim badai untuk merusak tanaman, dan kadang mencuri anak manusia serta meninggalkan changeling buruk rupa sebagai gantinya. Dalam satu kisah, seorang nenek mengalahkan sekawanan spriggan dengan membalik pakaiannya. Berdasarkan kumpulan Robert Hunt dan William Bottrell, Katharine Briggs menyebut spriggan pengawal para peri, dan English Dialect Dictionary (1905) membandingkannya dengan troll Skandinavia. Menurut artikel de, mereka disebut pencuri, perampok, dan penjahat dalam kepercayaan rakyat Cornwall.",
      "en": "Spriggans were known for foul tempers and loved making mischief for anyone who crossed them: they raised whirlwinds to scare travellers, sent storms to ruin crops and sometimes stole human children, leaving ugly changelings behind. One story tells of an old woman who outwitted a troop of spriggans simply by wearing her clothes inside out. Drawing on the collections of Robert Hunt and William Bottrell, Katharine Briggs called them fairy bodyguards, and the English Dialect Dictionary (1905) likened them to Scandinavian trolls. According to the de article Cornish belief calls them thieves, robbers and villains.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "spriggan-c04",
        "spriggan-c05",
        "spriggan-c06",
        "spriggan-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Am Fear Liath Mòr (\"Lelaki Kelabu Besar\") adalah sosok atau kriptid dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia yang konon menghantui puncak dan celah Ben Macdui di Cairngorms.",
    "en": "Am Fear Liath Mòr ('Big Grey Man') is a presence or cryptid of Scottish folklore said to haunt the summit and passes of Ben Macdui in the Cairngorms.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "am-fear-liath-mor-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore), Am Fear Liath Mòr (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic untuk \"Lelaki Kelabu Besar\"; juga Big Grey Man of Ben Macdui atau Greyman) adalah sosok atau kriptid yang konon menghantui puncak dan celah Ben Macdui, puncak tertinggi Cairngorms dan tertinggi kedua di Kepulauan Britania (British Isles) setelah Ben Nevis. Hanya sedikit saksi yang mengaku benar-benar melihatnya; ia dikatakan sangat kurus, lebih dari tiga meter, berkulit dan berambut gelap, berlengan panjang dan berbahu lebar. Lebih sering ia tak terlihat di dalam kabut, dan pertemuan hanya berupa bunyi kerikil berderak di belakang pendaki serta rasa gelisah.",
      "en": "Scottish folklore tells of Am Fear Liath Mòr, Gaelic for 'Big Grey Man' and also called the Big Grey Man of Ben Macdui or the Greyman: a presence or cryptid reputed to haunt the top and passes of Ben Macdui, the Cairngorms' highest summit and second only to Ben Nevis in the British Isles. Few witnesses claim to have actually seen it; it is said to be very thin, over three metres tall, dark-skinned and dark-haired, long-armed and broad-shouldered. More often it stays hidden in mountain mist, and encounters amount to crunching gravel behind climbers and a sense of dread.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "am-fear-liath-mor-c01",
        "am-fear-liath-mor-c02",
        "am-fear-liath-mor-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sosok ini banyak kemiripannya dengan Brenin Llwyd (\"Raja Kelabu\") dari mitologi Wales, yang juga setengah berwujud, diam, dan memakai kabut sebagai jubah untuk memangsa pelancong. Pada 1925 J. Norman Collie, profesor dan anggota Royal Geographical Society, memberi kisah pertama yang tercatat: pada 1891, saat turun dari puncak dalam kabut, ia mendengar langkah berderak di belakangnya dengan jarak tiga atau empat kali langkahnya sendiri, lalu lari ketakutan. Menurut artikel de, dalam tradisi Skotlandia ia dipandang sebagai fenomena gaib, tetapi kadang dibandingkan dengan Yeti Himalaya dan Bigfoot Amerika Utara.",
      "en": "The figure closely resembles the Brenin Llwyd ('Grey King') of Welsh myth, also semi-corporeal, silent and cloaked in mist to prey on travellers. In 1925 J. Norman Collie, a professor and member of the Royal Geographical Society, gave the first recorded account: in 1891, descending from the summit in mist, he heard crunching steps behind him three or four times the length of his own and fled in terror. According to the de article Scottish tradition treats it as a supernatural phenomenon, though it is sometimes compared with the Himalayan Yeti and North America's Bigfoot.",

    "id": "British big cats adalah sebutan untuk laporan penampakan kucing besar liar non-asli di Britania Raya, sering disebut \"panther\", \"puma\", atau \"kucing hitam\".",
    "en": "The term covers claimed sightings, across the United Kingdom, of big wild cats not native to Britain, often called 'panthers', 'pumas' or 'black cats'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "british-big-cats-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat dan legenda urban Britania (British folklore), British big cats merujuk pada laporan penampakan kucing besar liar non-asli di Britania Raya (United Kingdom), yang banyak digambarkan sebagai \"panther\", \"puma\", atau \"black cats\". Ada beberapa kasus langka hewan yang ditemukan, sering spesies berukuran sedang seperti lynx Eurasia, dan pada 1980 seekor puma tertangkap hidup di Skotlandia (Scotland). Hewan-hewan ini umumnya diduga peliharaan eksotis ilegal yang kabur atau dilepas, mungkin setelah Dangerous Wild Animals Act 1976.",
      "en": "In British folklore and urban legend, 'British big cats' means reported sightings of wild, non-native big cats in the United Kingdom, often described as 'panthers', 'pumas' or 'black cats'. A few rare animals have actually been recovered, mostly mid-sized species such as the Eurasian lynx, and in 1980 a puma was caught alive in Scotland. These are usually thought to be illegally kept exotic pets that escaped or were set loose, perhaps after the Dangerous Wild Animals Act 1976.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "british-big-cats-c01",
        "british-big-cats-c02",
        "british-big-cats-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namun keberadaan populasi \"kucing besar sejati\", apalagi yang berkembang biak, ditolak para ahli dan pemerintah Britania karena tidak ada bukti meyakinkan; penampakan dari jauh kebanyakan dianggap kucing rumahan yang salah dikira hewan besar. Seorang folklorist menyebutnya \"artefak media\". Dalam tradisi lebih tua, puisi Wales abad pertengahan Pa Gwr dalam Black Book of Carmarthen menyebut Cath Palug, \"kucing Palug\", yang berkeliaran di Anglesey sampai dibunuh Cei. Menurut artikel es, ada juga teori bahwa hewan-hewan itu fauna sisa Zaman Es.",
      "en": "Yet the idea of a population of 'true big cats', still less a breeding one, has been rejected by experts and the British government for lack of convincing proof; distant sightings are mostly put down to house cats mistaken for something larger. One folklorist called the sightings a 'media artifact'. Older tradition has the medieval Welsh poem Pa Gwr in the Black Book of Carmarthen naming Cath Palug, 'Palug's cat', which roamed Anglesey until Cei killed it. According to the es article some theories even hold them to be survivors from the Ice Age.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "british-big-cats-c03",
        "british-big-cats-c04",
        "british-big-cats-c05",
        "british-big-cats-c06"
      ]
    }

      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Populasi kucing besar sejati ditolak ahli dan pemerintah Britania (British government).",
        "en": "A population of true big cats is rejected by experts and the British government."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "british-big-cats-c04",
      "source_id": "british-big-cats-s1",
      "quote": "One folklorist considered such sightings of creatures to be a \"media artifact\" driven by British journalistic practices in the 1970s and 1980s",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seorang folklorist menyebutnya artefak media.",
        "en": "One folklorist called them a media artifact."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "british-big-cats-c05",
      "source_id": "british-big-cats-s1",
      "quote": "A medieval Welsh poem Pa Gwr in the Black Book of Carmarthen mentions a Cath Palug, meaning \"Palug's cat\" or \"clawing cat\", which roamed Anglesey until slain by Cei.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi Wales (Welsh) Pa Gwr dalam Black Book of Carmarthen: Cath Palug di Anglesey dibunuh Cei.",
        "en": "The Welsh poem Pa Gwr in the Black Book of Carmarthen: Cath Palug roamed Anglesey until slain by Cei."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "british-big-cats-c06",
      "source_id": "british-big-cats-s2",
      "quote": "Su existencia no ha sido comprobada, pero existen muchas teorías que intentan explicar cómo estos animales pudieron haber llegado a Gran Bretaña, incluyendo que son animales liberados después de que el Acta de animales salvajes peligrosos de 1976 entrara en vigor, o que son fauna superviviente de La Edad de Hielo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keberadaannya belum terbukti; teori: dilepas setelah Dangerous Wild Animals Act 1976 atau fauna sisa Zaman Es (Edad de Hielo); Britania Raya (Gran Bretaña).",
        "en": "Their existence is unproven; theories: released after the Dangerous Wild Animals Act 1976, or surviving Ice Age (Edad de Hielo) fauna in Great Britain (Gran Bretaña)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "british-big-cats-c07",
      "source_id": "british-big-cats-s2",
      "quote": "Este puma fue capturado en los bosques del condado de Inverness, Escocia en 1980. Se cree era una mascota abandonada.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puma ditangkap di hutan Inverness, Skotlandia (Escocia), 1980, diduga peliharaan terlantar.",
        "en": "This puma was caught in the woods of Inverness, Scotland (Escocia), in 1980, thought an abandoned pet."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jenny Greenteeth adalah nenek sihir sungai dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang menarik anak-anak atau orang tua ke dalam air dan menenggelamkan mereka.",
    "en": "Jenny Greenteeth is a river-hag of English folklore who pulls children or the elderly into the water and drowns them.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jenny-greenteeth-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jenny Greenteeth, juga Wicked Jenny, Ginny Greenteeth, dan Grinteeth, adalah sosok dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore). Ia nenek sihir sungai, mirip Peg Powler dan berasal dari grindylow, yang menarik anak-anak atau orang tua ke dalam air dan menenggelamkan mereka. Nama itu juga dipakai untuk rumput kolam atau duckweed yang dapat menutupi permukaan air kecil sehingga menipu dan berbahaya, terutama bagi anak-anak; dengan arti ini nama tersebut umum di sekitar Liverpool dan Lancashire barat daya.",
      "en": "Jenny Greenteeth, also Wicked Jenny, Ginny Greenteeth and Grinteeth, is a figure of English folklore. A river-hag much like Peg Powler and derived from the grindylow, she dragged children or old people into the water and drowned them. Her name is also given to pondweed or duckweed, which can carpet a small pond so that it looks solid and becomes a trap, above all for careless children; in that sense the name is common round Liverpool and south-west Lancashire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jenny-greenteeth-c01",
        "jenny-greenteeth-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sering digambarkan berkulit hijau, berambut panjang, dan bergigi tajam. Di Lancashire dan Staffordshire utara ia disebut Jinny Greenteeth, sedangkan di Cheshire dan Shropshire Wicked Jenny, Ginny Greenteeth, atau Jeannie Greenteeth. Namanya berasal dari grindylow, yang juga disebut \"Grendith\" atau \"Grinteeth\". Ia kemungkinan diciptakan untuk menakuti anak-anak agar menjauhi perairan berbahaya, serupa Rusalka Slavia, Kappa Jepang, atau Bunyip Australia, meski sebagian folklorist percaya kisahnya mengingatkan pada praktik kurban. Menurut artikel de, di Cheshire dan Shropshire ia juga disebut Peg o’ Nell.",
      "en": "She is often pictured green-skinned, long-haired and sharp-toothed. In Lancashire and north Staffordshire she is Jinny Greenteeth, while in Cheshire and Shropshire she is Wicked Jenny, Ginny Greenteeth or Jeannie Greenteeth. Her name comes from the grindylow, also called 'Grendith' or 'Grinteeth'. She was likely invented to keep children from dangerous water, like the Slavic Rusalka, the Japanese Kappa or Australia's Bunyip, though some folklorists think the tale recalls sacrificial rites. According to the de article she is also called Peg o’ Nell in Cheshire and Shropshire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jenny-greenteeth-c03",
        "jenny-greenteeth-c04",
        "jenny-greenteeth-c05",
        "jenny-greenteeth-c06"
      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Korrigan adalah roh peri atau mirip kerdil dalam cerita rakyat Breton, yang dalam sebagian kisah berupa perempuan peri penghuni mata air yang memikat lelaki menuju kematian.",
    "en": "The korrigan is a fairy or dwarf-like spirit of Breton folklore, in some accounts a siren-like female fairy of springs who lures men to their deaths.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "korrigan-c01",
      "korrigan-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Breton (Breton folklore), Korrigan adalah roh peri atau mirip kerdil. Kata korrigan dalam bahasa Breton berarti \"kerdil kecil\" (korr \"kerdil\", ig pengecil, an akhiran sayang). Namanya berbeda-beda menurut tempat. Théodore de Villemarqué dalam Barzaz Breiz memakai istilah itu bergantian dengan \"peri\" dan membedakannya dari kerdil, sedangkan Walter Evans-Wentz dalam The Fairy Faith in Celtic Countries berpendapat bahwa di Morbihan tak ada pembedaan jelas antara korrigan dan kerdil.",
      "en": "In Breton folklore a Korrigan is a fairy or dwarfish spirit. In Breton the word means 'small dwarf' (korr dwarf, ig a diminutive, an an affectionate ending). Its name varies from place to place. Théodore de Villemarqué, in Barzaz Breiz, uses the word interchangeably with 'fairy' and sets them apart from dwarves, while Walter Evans-Wentz in The Fairy Faith in Celtic Countries held that Morbihan lore draws no clear line between korrigans and dwarves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "korrigan-c01",
        "korrigan-c02",
        "korrigan-c03",
        "korrigan-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penulis lain memakai istilah ini hanya untuk peri perempuan mirip siren yang menghuni mata air dan sungai, sangat cantik saat senja atau malam tetapi pada siang hari bermata merah, berambut putih, dan berkulit keriput. Mereka kadang digambarkan sebagai putri atau druid perempuan yang menentang Kristen (Christianity) ketika para Rasul datang ke Brittany. Mereka dapat meramal, berubah wujud, dan bergerak secepat kilat; seperti siren mereka bernyanyi dan menyisir rambut panjang, membuat lelaki jatuh cinta lalu membunuhnya, dan suka mencuri anak manusia serta menggantinya dengan changeling. Menurut artikel de, mereka juga dikatakan dapat menyembuhkan penyakit yang tak tersembuhkan.",
      "en": "Other writers keep the name for siren-like female fairies of springs and rivers, lovely at dusk or night but by day red-eyed, white-haired and wrinkled. They are sometimes cast as princesses or druidesses who resisted Christianity when the Apostles came to convert Brittany. They foretell the future, change shape and move like lightning; like sirens they sing and comb their long hair, make men love them and then kill them, and like to steal human children, leaving changelings. According to the de article they are also said to cure otherwise incurable illnesses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "korrigan-c05",
        "korrigan-c06",
        "korrigan-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  "modern_depictions": [],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "korrigan-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korrigan",
      "title": "Korrigan",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",

    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-s2",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korrigan",
      "title": "Korrigan",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "de",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c01",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "In Breton folklore, a Korrigan ( pronounced [kɔˈriːɡãn] ) is a fairy or dwarf-like spirit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Breton (Breton folklore), Korrigan roh peri atau mirip kerdil; di Prancis.",
        "en": "In Breton folklore a Korrigan is a fairy or dwarf-like spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c02",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "The word korrigan means in Breton \"small-dwarf\" (korr means dwarf, ig is a diminutive and the suffix an is a hypocoristic).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korrigan berarti kerdil kecil dalam Breton (korr kerdil, ig pengecil).",
        "en": "Korrigan means small dwarf in Breton (korr dwarf, ig diminutive)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c03",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "Théodore de Villemarqué in Barzaz Breiz uses the term interchangeably with \"fairy\" and distinguishes them from dwarves (\"nains\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Théodore de Villemarqué (Barzaz Breiz) memakainya setara peri, beda dari kerdil.",
        "en": "Théodore de Villemarqué (Barzaz Breiz) uses it for fairy, distinct from dwarves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c04",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "However, they give themselves away when they cannot enumerate the full list of the days of the week (because of the sacredness of the full week).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka membuka kedok bila tak mampu menyebut seluruh hari dalam seminggu.",
        "en": "They give themselves away when they cannot list all the days of the week."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c05",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "Other authors use the term only to refer to siren-like female fairies who inhabit springs and rivers, \"lovely lustful golden-haired women who tried to lure men into their beds – and into a watery death\". These creatures are very beautiful when seen at dusk or night, but by day their eyes are red, their hair white, and their skin wrinkled",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peri perempuan mirip siren (Siren) di mata air dan sungai, memikat lelaki ke kematian; cantik malam, siang bermata merah, rambut putih, keriput.",
        "en": "Siren-like female fairies of springs and rivers luring men to death; lovely at night, by day red-eyed, white-haired, wrinkled."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c06",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "They are sometimes described as important princesses or druidesses who were opposed to Christianity when the Apostles came to convert Brittany. They hate priests, churches, and especially the Virgin Mary. They can predict the future, change shape, and move at lightning speed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Putri atau druid perempuan yang menentang Kristen (Christianity) di Brittany (Apostles, Rasul; Virgin Mary); meramal, berubah wujud, cepat seperti kilat; mencuri anak, changeling (Changeling).",
        "en": "Princesses or druidesses opposed to Christianity in Brittany; they predict the future, change shape and move at lightning speed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c07",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s2",
      "quote": "Die Korriganen sind Feen oder zwergenhafte Spukgestalten aus der keltischen Mythologie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korriganen peri (Feen) atau sosok hantu kerdil (zwergenhafte Spukgestalten) mitologi Kelt (keltischen).",
        "en": "The Korriganen are fairies (Feen) or dwarf-like spectres (zwergenhafte Spukgestalten) of Celtic (keltischen) myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c08",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s2",
      "quote": "Weiterhin werden ihnen magische Fähigkeiten nachgesagt: So sollen sie in die Zukunft blicken, unheilbare Krankheiten kurieren, jederzeit an jedem beliebigen Ort erscheinen und sogar jede beliebige Gestalt annehmen können.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka meramal, menyembuhkan penyakit tak tersembuhkan (unheilbare Krankheiten), muncul di mana saja, berubah wujud.",
        "en": "They foresee the future, cure incurable illnesses (unheilbare Krankheiten), appear anywhere and take any shape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "korrigan-c09",
      "source_id": "korrigan-s1",
      "quote": "In contrast Walter Evans-Wentz in The Fairy Faith in Celtic Countries argued that in the mythology of Morbihan there is no clear distinction between korrigans and nains",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Walter Evans-Wentz (The Fairy Faith in Celtic Countries): di Morbihan tak ada beda jelas korrigan dan kerdil.",
        "en": "Walter Evans-Wentz (The Fairy Faith in Celtic Countries): in Morbihan no clear distinction between korrigans and dwarves."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Morag adalah julukan monster danau yang diyakini banyak orang hidup di Loch Morar, Skotlandia, dan termasuk monster legenda Skotlandia yang paling banyak ditulis setelah Nessie.",
    "en": "Morag is the nickname of a loch monster many believe lives in Loch Morar, Scotland, among the most written-about Scottish monsters after Nessie.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "morag-lake-monster-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Morag (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic: Mòrag) adalah julukan monster danau yang diyakini banyak orang hidup di Loch Morar, Skotlandia (Scotland). Setelah Nessie, ia termasuk monster legenda Skotlandia yang paling banyak ditulis. \"Morag\", nama perempuan Skotlandia, adalah permainan kata atas nama danau itu. Laporan penampakan berawal sejak 1887 dan mencapai 34 kejadian pada 1981, enam belas di antaranya dengan banyak saksi.",
      "en": "Morag (Scottish Gaelic Mòrag) is the nickname of a loch monster that many people believe lives in Loch Morar, Scotland. Only Nessie has drawn more pages among Scotland's monsters of legend. 'Morag', a Scottish girl's name, puns on the loch's name. Reports go back to 1887 and had reached 34 incidents by 1981, sixteen with several witnesses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "morag-lake-monster-c01",
        "morag-lake-monster-c02",
        "morag-lake-monster-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Klaim yang banyak diberitakan dari Agustus 1969 melibatkan dua lelaki setempat, Duncan McDonell dan William Simpson, yang mengaku perahunya tak sengaja menabrak makhluk itu sehingga ia menyerang; McDonell menangkis dengan dayung dan Simpson menembaknya dengan senapan, lalu makhluk itu tenggelam perlahan. Mereka menggambarkannya berwarna cokelat, panjang 7,6–9,1 m, berkulit kasar, dengan tiga punuk di punggung. Menurut artikel de, Mhorag (\"roh danau\" dalam bahasa Gaelik) digambarkan mirip plesiosaurus dan sangat mirip monster Loch Ness.",
      "en": "A widely reported claim of August 1969 involved two local men, Duncan McDonell and William Simpson, who said their boat accidentally struck the creature and it attacked; McDonell fended it off with an oar and Simpson fired his rifle, after which it slowly sank. They described it as brown, 7.6–9.1 m long, rough-skinned, with three humps on its back. According to the de article Mhorag ('spirit of the loch' in Gaelic) is described as plesiosaur-like and very similar to the Loch Ness Monster.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "morag-lake-monster-c03",
        "morag-lake-monster-c04",
        "morag-lake-monster-c05"
      ]
    }

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Owlman adalah makhluk humanoid mirip burung hantu dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall yang konon terlihat pada 1976 di desa Mawnan, Cornwall.",
    "en": "The Owlman is an owl-like humanoid of Cornish folklore reportedly seen in 1976 in the village of Mawnan, Cornwall.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "owlman-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornish folklore), Owlman (Cornwall/Cornish: Kowanden), juga Cornish Owlman atau Owlman of Mawnan, adalah makhluk humanoid mirip burung hantu yang konon terlihat pada 1976 di desa Mawnan, Cornwall, Britania Raya (United Kingdom). Penampakan makhluk yang terbang di atas menara gereja setempat itu ditafsirkan sebagian orang sebagai salah kira terhadap burung hantu gudang. Kisah ini berasal dari Tony \"Doc\" Shiels, yang mengaku menyelidiki laporan dua gadis yang berlibur di Mawnan dan melihat makhluk bersayap besar melayang di atas menara gereja pada 17 April 1976.",
      "en": "Cornish folklore's Owlman (Cornish Kowanden), sometimes named after Mawnan or simply called the Cornish Owlman, is a humanoid with owl features, which people claimed to see in 1976 around Mawnan, a Cornish village in the United Kingdom. Sightings of it flying over the local church tower have been put down by some to mistaken barn owls. The story began with Tony 'Doc' Shiels, who said he looked into a report of two girls on holiday in Mawnan who saw a big winged creature hovering above the church tower on 17 April 1976.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "owlman-c01",
        "owlman-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Shiels, pada 3 Juli dua gadis 14 tahun, Sally Chapman dan Barbara Perry, yang sedang berkemah, melihat \"burung hantu besar bertelinga runcing, sebesar manusia\" dengan mata menyala dan cakar hitam seperti capit. Penampakan sporadis dilaporkan lagi pada 1978, 1979, 1989, dan 1995. Sejarawan okultisme Gareth Medway menduga seluruh kisah itu tipuan Shiels. Menurut artikel ru, Owlman dalam literatur kriptozoologi kadang dibandingkan dengan Mothman dari Amerika.",
      "en": "Shiels said that on 3 July two 14-year-old girls, Sally Chapman and Barbara Perry, camping nearby, met a man-sized owl with pointed ears, shining eyes and black pincer-like claws. Scattered sightings were reported again in 1978, 1979, 1989 and 1995. Occult historian Gareth Medway thought the whole tale a hoax by Shiels. According to the ru article the Owlman is sometimes compared in cryptozoological writing with America's Mothman.",

      "quote": "In Cornish folklore, the Owlman (Cornish: Kowanden ), also known as the Cornish Owlman or Owlman of Mawnan, is an owl-like humanoid creature reportedly sighted in 1976 in the village of Mawnan, Cornwall, United Kingdom. The reported sightings of the creature flying above the local church tower have been interpreted by some as misidentifications of a barn owl",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornish folklore), Owlman (Cornish: Kowanden; Cornish Owlman, Owlman of Mawnan) humanoid mirip burung hantu, terlihat 1976 di Mawnan, Cornwall, Britania Raya (United Kingdom); terbang di atas menara gereja; mungkin burung hantu gudang.",
        "en": "In Cornish folklore the Owlman (Cornish Kowanden; Cornish Owlman, Owlman of Mawnan) is an owl-like humanoid seen in 1976 at Mawnan, Cornwall, United Kingdom, flying over the church tower; perhaps a barn owl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "owlman-c02",
      "source_id": "owlman-s1",
      "quote": "The story originated with Tony \"Doc\" Shiels, who had been involved in a series of \"monster-raising\" exploits in 1976. Shiels claimed to have investigated a report of two young girls on holiday in Mawnan who allegedly saw a large winged creature hovering above the tower of St Mawnan and St Stephen's Church, Mawnan on 17 April 1976.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah dari Tony Doc Shiels; dua gadis melihat makhluk bersayap di atas menara gereja Mawnan, 17 April 1976. Kowanden",
        "en": "The story came from Tony Doc Shiels; two girls saw a winged creature above Mawnan church tower, 17 April 1976."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "owlman-c03",
      "source_id": "owlman-s1",
      "quote": "The account states that the two girls were camping when they encountered a creature described as a \"big owl with pointed ears, as big as a man\", with glowing eyes and black, pincer-like claws.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "3 Juli: Sally Chapman dan Barbara Perry melihat burung hantu besar bertelinga runcing sebesar manusia, mata menyala, cakar hitam.",
        "en": "3 July: Sally Chapman and Barbara Perry saw a big owl with pointed ears, as big as a man, glowing eyes, black claws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "owlman-c04",
      "source_id": "owlman-s1",
      "quote": "Sporadic reports of the Owlman sightings in the vicinity of the church were recorded in 1978, 1979, 1989, and 1995.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Sporadic sightings in 1978, 1979, 1989, 1995."

      "source_id": "owlman-s1",
